\displaystyle\mathsf{tr}\left(({\boldsymbol{X}}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{\,T}}+d\gamma\mathbf{I}_{n})^{-2}{\boldsymbol{X}}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{>k}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{\,T}}\right)\leq\lambda_{k+1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\widehat{\lambda}_{i}}{(d\gamma+\widehat{\lambda}_{i})^{2}}\leq\lambda_{k+1}\frac{n}{4d\gamma}\lesssim\frac{\lambda_{k+1}}{\gamma}

(159)

where
\widehat{\lambda}_{i}
are the eigenvalues of
{\boldsymbol{X}}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{\,T}}
. Here we use the fact that
\frac{t}{(r+t)^{2}}\leq\frac{1}{4r}
for all
t,r>0
. On the other hand,

\displaystyle\mathsf{tr}\left(({\boldsymbol{X}}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{\,T}}+d\gamma\mathbf{I}_{n})^{-2}{\boldsymbol{X}}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{\leq k}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{\,T}}\right)\leq\sum_{i\leq k}\lambda_{i}\left\|(d\gamma\boldsymbol{I}_{n}+{\boldsymbol{X}}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{\,T}})^{-1}{\boldsymbol{X}}{\boldsymbol{u}}_{i}\right\|^{2}\,.

(160)

Now, using the argument similar to that in
[
BLLT20
]
, we define
A_{-i}=d\gamma\boldsymbol{I}_{n}+{\boldsymbol{X}}(\boldsymbol{I}_{n}-{\boldsymbol{u}}_{i}{\boldsymbol{u}}_{i}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{\,T}}){\boldsymbol{X}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{\,T}}
,
{\boldsymbol{v}}={\boldsymbol{X}}{\boldsymbol{u}}_{i}
and write

\displaystyle\left\|(d\gamma\boldsymbol{I}_{n}+{\boldsymbol{X}}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{\,T}})^{-1}{\boldsymbol{X}}{\boldsymbol{u}}_{i}\right\|^{2}=\left\|(A_{-i}+{\boldsymbol{v}}{\boldsymbol{v}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{\,T}})^{-1}{\boldsymbol{v}}\right\|^{2}=\frac{{\boldsymbol{v}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{\,T}}A_{-i}^{-2}{\boldsymbol{v}}}{(1+{\boldsymbol{v}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{\,T}}A_{-i}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{v}})^{2}}

(161)

by the Sherman-Morrison formula. The last quantity is upper bounded by

\displaystyle\frac{1}{d\gamma}\frac{{\boldsymbol{v}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{\,T}}A_{-i}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{v}}}{(1+{\boldsymbol{v}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{\,T}}A_{-i}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{v}})^{2}}\leq\frac{1}{4\gamma d}.

(162)

Substituting in (
160
), we obtain an upper bound of

\frac{1}{4\gamma d}\sum_{i\leq k}\lambda_{i}\lesssim\frac{\lambda_{1}k}{\gamma n},

assuming
n\lesssim d
.
∎

A.2
Exact characterization in the proportional asymptotics

We will denote by
{\boldsymbol{K}}=(h(\langle{\boldsymbol{x}}_{i},{\boldsymbol{x}}_{j}\rangle/d))_{i,j\leq n}
the
kernel matrix. We will also denote by
{\boldsymbol{K}}_{1}
the linearized kernel

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{K}}_{1}

\displaystyle=\beta\frac{{\boldsymbol{X}}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{\,T}}}{d}+\beta\gamma\mathbf{I}_{n}+\alpha\mathbf{1}\mathbf{1}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{\,T}}\,,

(163)

\displaystyle\alpha

\displaystyle:=h(0)+h^{\prime\prime}(0)\frac{\mathsf{tr}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{2})}{2d^{2}},~\beta:=h^{\prime}(0),

(164)

\displaystyle\gamma

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{h^{\prime}(0)}\big[h(\mathsf{tr}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}})/d)-h(0)-h^{\prime}(0)\mathsf{tr}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}/d)\big].

(165)

Assumption
4.12
.

We assume that the coordinates of
{\boldsymbol{z}}={\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{-1/2}{\boldsymbol{x}}
are
independent, with zero mean and unit variance, so that

{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}=\mathbb{E}{\boldsymbol{x}}{\boldsymbol{x}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{\,T}}
.
Further assume there are constants

0<\eta,M<\infty
, such that the following hold.

(a)

For all
i\leq d
,

\mathbb{E}[|{\boldsymbol{z}}_{i}|^{8+\eta}]\leq M
.

(b)

\|{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}\|\leq M
,
d^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{d}\lambda_{i}^{-1}\leq M
, where
\lambda_{1},\dots,\lambda_{d}
are the eigenvalues of
{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}
.

Theorem
4.13
.

Let
0<M,\eta<\infty
be fixed constants and suppose that
Assumption
4.12
holds with
M^{-1}\leq d/n\leq M
. Further assume that
h
is continuous on
{\mathbb{R}}
and smooth in a
neighborhood of
0
with
h(0)
,
h^{\prime}(0)>0
, that
\|f^{*}\|_{L^{4+\eta}(\mathbb{P})}\leq M
and that the
z_{i}
’s are
M
-sub-Gaussian.
Let
y_{i}=f^{*}({\boldsymbol{x}}_{i})+\xi_{i}
,
\mathbb{E}(\xi_{i}^{2})=\sigma_{\xi}^{2}
,
and
{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0}:={\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{-1}\mathbb{E}[{\boldsymbol{x}}f^{*}({\boldsymbol{x}})]
.
Let
\lambda_{*}>0
be the unique positive solution of

\displaystyle n\Big(1-\frac{\gamma}{\lambda_{*}}\Big)={\mathsf{tr}}\Big({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{*}{\mathbf{I}})^{-1}\Big)\,.

(166)

Define
\mathscrsfs{B}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}},{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0})
and
\mathscrsfs{V}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}})
by

\displaystyle\mathscrsfs{V}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}})

\displaystyle:=\frac{{\mathsf{tr}}\big({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{2}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{*}{\mathbf{I}})^{-2}\big)}{n-{\mathsf{tr}}\big({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{2}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{*}{\mathbf{I}})^{-2}\big)}\,,

(167)

\displaystyle\mathscrsfs{B}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}},{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0})

\displaystyle:=\frac{\lambda_{*}^{2}\langle{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0},({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{*}{\mathbf{I}})^{-2}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0}\rangle}{1-n^{-1}{\mathsf{tr}}\big({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{2}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{*}{\mathbf{I}})^{-2}\big)}\,.

(168)

Finally, let
{\widehat{\textsc{bias}}^{2}}
and
{\widehat{\textsc{var}}}
denote the
squared bias and variance for the minimum-norm interpolant.
Then there exist
C,c_{0}>0
(depending also on the constants in Assumption
4.12
)
such that the following holds with probability at least
1-Cn^{-1/4}

(here
{\mathsf{P}}_{>1}
denotes the projector orthogonal to affine
functions in
L^{2}(\mathbb{P})
):

\displaystyle\big|{\widehat{\textsc{bias}}^{2}}-\mathscrsfs{B}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}},{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0})-\|{\mathsf{P}}_{>1}f^{*}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}(1+\mathscrsfs{V}(\Sigma))\big|

\displaystyle\leq Cn^{-c_{0}}\,,

(169)

\displaystyle\big|{\widehat{\textsc{var}}}-\sigma_{\xi}^{2}\mathscrsfs{V}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}})\big|

\displaystyle\leq Cn^{-c_{0}}\,.

(170)

Remark A.1
.

The result for the variance will be proved under weaker
assumptions and in a stronger form than stated. In particular, it does not require any assumption on the target function
f_{*}
, and it holds with smaller error terms than stated.

Remark A.2
.

Notice that by positive definiteness of the kernel, we have
h^{\prime}(0),h^{\prime\prime}(0)\geq 0
. Hence the conditions that these are strictly positive
is essentially a non-degeneracy requirement.

We note for future reference that the target function
f^{*}
is decomposed as

\displaystyle f^{*}({\boldsymbol{x}})=b_{0}+\langle{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0},{\boldsymbol{x}}\rangle+{\mathsf{P}}_{>1}f^{*}({\boldsymbol{x}})\,,

(171)

where
b_{0}:=\mathbb{E}\{f^{*}({\boldsymbol{x}})\}
,
{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0}:={\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{-1}\mathbb{E}[{\boldsymbol{x}}f^{*}({\boldsymbol{x}})]
as defined above and
\mathbb{E}\{{\mathsf{P}}_{>1}f^{*}({\boldsymbol{x}})\}
,

\mathbb{E}\{{\boldsymbol{x}}{\mathsf{P}}_{>1}f^{*}({\boldsymbol{x}})\}={\boldsymbol{0}}
.

A.2.1
Preliminaries

Throughout the proof, we will use
C
for constants that depend uniquely on the constants in Assumption

4.12
and Theorem
4.13
. We also write that an inequality holds
with very high probability

if, for any
A>0
, we can choose the constants
C
in the inequality such that this holds with probability at least
1-n^{-A}

for all
A
large enough.

We will repeatedly use the following bound, see e.g.
[
EK10
]
.

Lemma A.2
.

Under the assumptions of Theorem
4.13
, we have, with very high probability

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{K}}={\boldsymbol{K}}_{1}+{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}\,,\;\;\;\;\;\|{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}\|\leq n^{-c_{0}}\,.

(172)

In particular, as long as
h
is non-linear, we have
{\boldsymbol{K}}\succeq c_{*}{\mathbf{I}}_{n}
,
c_{*}=\beta\gamma>0
with probability at least
1-Cn^{-D}
.

Define the matrix
{\boldsymbol{M}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n\times n}
, and the vector
{\boldsymbol{v}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
by

\displaystyle M_{ij}

\displaystyle:=\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{x}}}\left\{h\Big(\frac{1}{d}\langle{\boldsymbol{x}}_{i},{\boldsymbol{x}}\rangle\Big)h\Big(\frac{1}{d}\langle{\boldsymbol{x}}_{j},{\boldsymbol{x}}\rangle\Big)\right\}\,,

(173)

\displaystyle v_{i}

\displaystyle:=\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{x}}}\left\{h\Big(\frac{1}{d}\langle{\boldsymbol{x}}_{i},{\boldsymbol{x}}\rangle\Big)f^{*}({\boldsymbol{x}})\right\}\,.

(174)

Our first lemma provides useful approximations of these quantities.

Lemma A.3
.

Define (here expectations are over
G\sim{\sf N}(0,1)
):

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{v}}_{0}

\displaystyle:={\boldsymbol{a}}_{0}b_{0}+\frac{1}{d}h^{\prime}(0){\boldsymbol{X}}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0}\,,

(175)

\displaystyle a_{i,0}

\displaystyle:=\mathbb{E}\Big\{h\Big(\sqrt{\frac{Q_{ii}}{d}}\,G\Big)\Big\}\,,\;\;\;\;Q_{ij}:=\frac{1}{d}\langle{\boldsymbol{x}}_{i},{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}{\boldsymbol{x}}_{j}\rangle\,.

(176)

and

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{M}}_{0}

\displaystyle:={\boldsymbol{a}}{\boldsymbol{a}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{\,T}}}+{\boldsymbol{B}}\,,\;\;\;\;\;\;{\boldsymbol{B}}:=\frac{1}{d}\,{\boldsymbol{D}}{\boldsymbol{Q}}{\boldsymbol{D}}\,,

(177)

\displaystyle a_{i}

\displaystyle:=a_{i,0}+a_{i,1}\,,\;\;\;\;a_{i,1}=\frac{1}{6}\Big(\frac{Q_{ii}}{d}\Big)^{3/2}h^{(3)}(0)\sum_{j=1}^{d}\frac{({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{1/2}{\boldsymbol{x}}_{i})_{j}^{3}}{\|{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{1/2}{\boldsymbol{x}}_{i}\|_{2}^{3}}\mathbb{E}(z_{j}^{3})\,,

(178)

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{D}}

\displaystyle:={\rm diag}(D_{1},\dots,D_{n})\,,\;\;\;\;\;\;D_{i}:=\mathbb{E}\Big\{h^{\prime}\Big(\sqrt{\frac{Q_{ii}}{d}}\,G\Big)\Big\}\,.

(179)

Then the following hold with very high probability
(in other words, for any
A>0
there exists
C
such that the following hold with probability at least
1-n^{-A}
for all
n
large enough)

\displaystyle\max_{i\leq n}\big|v_{i}-v_{0,i}\big|

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{\sqrt{\log d}}{d^{3/2}}\,,

(180)

\displaystyle\max_{i\neq j\leq n}\big|M_{ij}-M_{0,ij}\big|

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{\log d}{d^{5/2}}\,,

(181)

\displaystyle\max_{i\leq n}\big|M_{ii}-M_{0,ii}\big|

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{\log d}{d^{2}}\,.

(182)

In particular, this implies
\|{\boldsymbol{v}}-{\boldsymbol{v}}_{0}\|_{2}\leq Cd^{-1}\sqrt{\log d}
,
\|{\boldsymbol{M}}-{\boldsymbol{M}}_{0}\|_{F}\leq Cd^{-3/2}\log d
.

Proof.

Throughout the proof we will work on the intersection
{\mathcal{E}}_{1}\cap{\mathcal{E}}_{2}

of following events, which hold with very high probability by standard concentration arguments. These events are defined by

\displaystyle{\mathcal{E}}_{1}

\displaystyle:=\Big\{C^{-1}\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\|{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}{\boldsymbol{z}}_{i}\|_{2}\leq C;\;\;\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\|{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}{\boldsymbol{z}}_{i}\|_{\infty}\leq C\sqrt{\frac{\log d}{d}}\;\;\forall i\leq n\Big\}

(183)

\displaystyle=\Big\{C^{-2}\leq\frac{1}{d}\langle{\boldsymbol{x}}_{i},{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}{\boldsymbol{x}}_{i}\rangle\leq C^{2};\;\;\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\|{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{1/2}{\boldsymbol{x}}_{i}\|_{\infty}\leq C\sqrt{\frac{\log d}{d}}\;\;\forall i\leq n\Big\}\,,

(184)

and

\displaystyle{\mathcal{E}}_{2}

\displaystyle:=\Big\{\frac{1}{d}\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}{\boldsymbol{z}}_{i})_{\ell}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}{\boldsymbol{z}}_{j})^{2}_{\ell}\leq\frac{\log d}{d^{1/2}}\,;\;\;\frac{1}{d}|\langle{\boldsymbol{z}}_{i},{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}{\boldsymbol{z}}_{j}\rangle|\leq C\sqrt{\frac{\log d}{d}}\,;\;\;\frac{1}{d}|\langle{\boldsymbol{z}}_{i},{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{2}{\boldsymbol{z}}_{j}\rangle|\leq C\sqrt{\frac{\log d}{d}}\;\;\forall i\neq j\leq n\Big\}

(185)

\displaystyle=\Big\{\frac{1}{d}\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{1/2}{\boldsymbol{x}}_{i})_{\ell}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{1/2}{\boldsymbol{x}}_{j})^{2}_{\ell}\leq\frac{\log d}{d^{1/2}}\,;\;\;\frac{1}{d}|\langle{\boldsymbol{x}}_{i},{\boldsymbol{x}}_{j}\rangle|\leq C\sqrt{\frac{\log d}{d}}\,\;\;\frac{1}{d}|\langle{\boldsymbol{x}}_{i},{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}{\boldsymbol{x}}_{j}\rangle|\leq C\sqrt{\frac{\log d}{d}}\;\;\forall i\neq j\leq n\Big\}\,.

Recall that, by assumption,
h
is smooth on an interval
[-t_{0},t_{0}]
,
t_{0}>0
.
On the event
{\mathcal{E}}_{2}
,
we have
\langle{\boldsymbol{x}}_{i},{\boldsymbol{x}}_{j}\rangle/d\in[-t_{0},t_{0}]
for all
i\neq j
.
If
h
is not smooth everywhere, we
can always modify it outside
[-t_{0}/2,t_{0}/2]
to obtain a kernel
\tilde{h}
that is smooth everywhere.
Since
{\boldsymbol{x}}
is sub-Gaussian, as long as
\|{\boldsymbol{x}}_{i}\|/\sqrt{d}\leq C
for all
i\leq n
(this happens on
{\mathcal{E}}_{1}
)
we have (for
{\boldsymbol{x}}\sim\mathbb{P}
),
\langle{\boldsymbol{x}}_{i},{\boldsymbol{x}}\rangle/d\in[-t_{0}/2,t_{0}/2]
with probability at least
1-e^{-d/C}
. Further using the
fact that
f
is bounded in Eqs. (
173
), (
174
), we get,

\displaystyle M_{ij}

\displaystyle:=\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{x}}}\left\{\tilde{h}\Big(\frac{1}{d}\langle{\boldsymbol{x}}_{i},{\boldsymbol{x}}\rangle\Big)\tilde{h}\Big(\frac{1}{d}\langle{\boldsymbol{x}}_{j},{\boldsymbol{x}}\rangle\Big)\right\}+O(e^{-d/C})\,,

(186)

\displaystyle v_{i}

\displaystyle:=\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{x}}}\left\{\tilde{h}\Big(\frac{1}{d}\langle{\boldsymbol{x}}_{i},{\boldsymbol{x}}\rangle\Big)f^{*}({\boldsymbol{x}})\right\}+O(e^{-d/C})\,,

(187)

where the term
O(e^{-d/C})
is uniform over
i,j\leq n
. Analogously, in the definition of
{\boldsymbol{v}}_{0}
,
{\boldsymbol{M}}_{0}

(more precisely, in defining
{\boldsymbol{a}}_{0}
,
{\boldsymbol{D}}
), we can replace
h
by
\tilde{h}
at the price of an
O(e^{-d/C})
error.
Since these error terms are negligible as compared to the ones in the statement, we shall hereafter neglect them
and set
\tilde{h}=h
(which corresponds to
defining arbitrarily the derivatives of
h
outside a neighborhood of
0
).

We denote by
h_{i,k}
the
k
-th coefficient of
h((Q_{ii}/d)^{1/2}x)
in the basis of Hermite polynomials.
Namely:

\displaystyle h_{i,k}=\mathbb{E}\Big\{h\Big(\sqrt{\frac{Q_{ii}}{d}}\,G\Big)\,{\rm He}_{k}(G)\Big\}=\Big(\frac{Q_{ii}}{d}\Big)^{k/2}\mathbb{E}\Big\{h^{(k)}\Big(\sqrt{\frac{Q_{ii}}{d}}\,G\Big)\Big\}\,.

(188)

Here
h^{(k)}
denotes the
k
-th derivative of
h
(recall that by the argument above we can assume, without loss of generality, that

h
is
k
-times differentiable).

We write
h_{i,>k}
for the remainder after the first
k

terms of the Hermite expansion have been removed:

\displaystyle h_{i,>k}\Big(\sqrt{\frac{Q_{ii}}{d}}\,x\Big)

\displaystyle:=h\Big(\sqrt{\frac{Q_{ii}}{d}}\,x\Big)-\sum_{\ell=0}^{k}\frac{1}{\ell!}h_{i,\ell}\,{\rm He}_{\ell}(x)

(189)

\displaystyle=h\Big(\sqrt{\frac{Q_{ii}}{d}}\,x\Big)-\sum_{\ell=0}^{k}\frac{1}{\ell!}\Big(\frac{Q_{ii}}{d}\Big)^{\ell/2}\mathbb{E}\Big\{h^{(k)}\Big(\sqrt{\frac{Q_{ii}}{d}}\,G\Big)\Big\}\,{\rm He}_{\ell}(x)\,.

Finally, we denote by
\hat{h}_{i,>k}(x)
the remainder after the first
k
terms in the Taylor expansion have been subtracted:

\displaystyle\hat{h}_{>k}(x)

\displaystyle:=h(x)-\sum_{\ell=0}^{k}\frac{1}{\ell!}h^{(\ell)}(0)\,x^{\ell}\,.

(190)

Of course
h-\hat{h}_{>k}
is a polynomial of degree
k
, and therefore its projection orthogonal to the first
k

Hermite polynomials vanishes, whence

\displaystyle h_{i,>k}\Big(\sqrt{\frac{Q_{ii}}{d}}\,x\Big)

\displaystyle=\hat{h}_{>k}\Big(\sqrt{\frac{Q_{ii}}{d}}\,x\Big)-\sum_{\ell=0}^{k}\frac{1}{\ell!}\Big(\frac{Q_{ii}}{d}\Big)^{\ell/2}\mathbb{E}\Big\{\hat{h}_{>k}^{(\ell)}\Big(\sqrt{\frac{Q_{ii}}{d}}\,G\Big)\Big\}\,{\rm He}_{\ell}(x)\,.

(191)

Note that, by smoothness of
h
, we have
|\hat{h}_{>k}^{(\ell)}(t)|\leq C\min(|t|^{k+1-\ell},1)
, and therefore

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{\ell!}\Big(\frac{Q_{ii}}{d}\Big)^{\ell/2}\mathbb{E}\Big\{\hat{h}_{>k}^{(\ell)}\Big(\sqrt{\frac{Q_{ii}}{d}}\,G\Big)\Big\}\right|\leq Cd^{-(k+1)/2}\,.

(192)

We also have that
|\hat{h}_{>k}(t)|\leq C\min(1,|t|^{k+1})
.
Define
{\boldsymbol{v}}_{i}={\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{1/2}{\boldsymbol{x}}_{i}/\sqrt{d}
,
\|{\boldsymbol{v}}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}=Q_{ii}
. For any fixed
m\geq 2
,
by Eq. (
191
) and the triangle inequality,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{z}}}\Big\{\Big|h_{i,>k}\Big(\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\langle{\boldsymbol{v}}_{i},{\boldsymbol{z}}\rangle\Big)\Big|^{m}\Big\}^{1/m}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{\leq}}\mathbb{E}\Big\{\Big|\hat{h}_{>k}\Big(\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\langle{\boldsymbol{v}}_{i},{\boldsymbol{z}}\rangle\Big)\Big|^{m}\Big\}^{1/m}+Cd^{-(k+1)/2}\sum_{\ell=0}^{k}\mathbb{E}\Big\{\Big|{\rm He}_{\ell}\Big(\frac{\langle{\boldsymbol{v}}_{i},{\boldsymbol{z}}\rangle}{\|{\boldsymbol{v}}_{i}\|_{2}}\Big)\Big|^{m}\Big\}^{1/m}

\displaystyle\leq C\,\Big(\frac{Q_{ii}}{d}\Big)^{(k+1)/2}+C\,d^{-(k+1)/2}\leq C\,d^{-(k+1)/2}\,,

(193)

where the inequality
(a)
follows since
\langle{\boldsymbol{v}}_{i},{\boldsymbol{z}}\rangle
is

C
-sub-Gaussian.
Note that Eqs. (
189
), (
193
) can also be rewritten as

\displaystyle h\Big(\frac{1}{d}\langle{\boldsymbol{x}}_{i},{\boldsymbol{x}}\rangle\Big)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\ell=0}^{k}\frac{1}{\ell!}h_{i,\ell}\,{\rm He}_{\ell}\Big(\frac{1}{\sqrt{dQ_{ii}}}\langle{\boldsymbol{x}}_{i},{\boldsymbol{x}}\rangle\Big)+h_{i,>k}\Big(\frac{1}{d}\langle{\boldsymbol{x}}_{i},{\boldsymbol{x}}\rangle\Big)\,,

(194)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big\{\Big|h_{i,>k}\Big(\frac{1}{d}\langle{\boldsymbol{x}}_{i},{\boldsymbol{x}}\rangle\Big)\Big|^{m}\}^{1/m}

\displaystyle\leq C\,d^{-(k+1)/2}\,.

(195)

We next prove Eq. (
180
). Using Eq. (
194
) with
k=2
and recalling
{\rm He}_{0}(x)=1
,

{\rm He}_{1}(x)=x
,
{\rm He}_{2}(x)=x^{2}-1
, we get

\displaystyle v_{i}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{x}}}\left\{h\Big(\frac{1}{d}\langle{\boldsymbol{x}}_{i},{\boldsymbol{x}}\rangle\Big)f^{*}({\boldsymbol{x}})\right\}

\displaystyle=h_{i,0}\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{x}}}\left\{f^{*}({\boldsymbol{x}})\right\}+\frac{h_{i,1}}{\sqrt{dQ_{ii}}}\langle{\boldsymbol{x}}_{i},\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{x}}}\left\{{\boldsymbol{x}}f^{*}({\boldsymbol{x}})\right\}\rangle

\displaystyle\qquad{}+\frac{h_{i,2}}{2dQ_{ii}}\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{x}}}\left\{f^{*}({\boldsymbol{x}})(\langle{\boldsymbol{x}},{\boldsymbol{x}}_{i}\rangle^{2}-dQ_{ii})\right\}+\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{x}}}\left\{h_{i,>2}\Big(\frac{1}{d}\langle{\boldsymbol{x}}_{i},{\boldsymbol{x}}\rangle\Big)f^{*}({\boldsymbol{x}})\right\}

\displaystyle=h_{i,0}b_{0}+\frac{h_{i,1}}{\sqrt{dQ_{ii}}}\langle{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0},{\boldsymbol{x}}_{i}\rangle+\frac{h_{i,2}}{2dQ_{ii}}\langle{\boldsymbol{x}}_{i},{\boldsymbol{F}}_{2}{\boldsymbol{x}}_{i}\rangle+\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{x}}}\left\{h_{i,>2}\Big(\frac{1}{d}\langle{\boldsymbol{x}}_{i},{\boldsymbol{x}}\rangle\Big)f^{*}({\boldsymbol{x}})\right\}\,.

Here we defined the
d\times d
matrix
{\boldsymbol{F}}_{2}=\mathbb{E}\{[f^{*}({\boldsymbol{x}})-b_{0}]{\boldsymbol{x}}{\boldsymbol{x}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{\,T}}}\}
.
Recalling the definitions of
h_{i,k}
, in Eq. (
188
), we get
h_{i,0}=a_{i,0}
.
Comparing other terms we obtain that the following holds with very high probability,

\displaystyle|v_{i}-v_{0,i}|\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{d}\Big|\mathbb{E}\Big\{h^{\prime}\Big(\sqrt{\frac{Q_{ii}}{d}}\,G\Big)\Big\}-h^{\prime}(0)\Big|\cdot|\langle{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0},{\boldsymbol{x}}_{i}\rangle|+\frac{1}{d^{2}}\Big|\mathbb{E}\Big\{h^{\prime\prime}\Big(\sqrt{\frac{Q_{ii}}{d}}\,G\Big)\Big\}\Big|\cdot\Big|\langle{\boldsymbol{x}}_{i},{\boldsymbol{F}}_{2}{\boldsymbol{x}}_{i}\rangle\Big|

\displaystyle+\Big|\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{x}}}\left\{h_{i,>2}\Big(\frac{1}{d}\langle{\boldsymbol{x}}_{i},{\boldsymbol{x}}\rangle\Big)f^{*}({\boldsymbol{x}})\right\}\Big|

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{\leq}}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{d}\times\frac{C}{d}\times C\log d+\frac{C}{d^{2}}\big|\langle{\boldsymbol{x}}_{i},{\boldsymbol{F}}_{2}{\boldsymbol{x}}_{i}\rangle\big|+C\,d^{-3/2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C}{d^{2}}\Big|\langle{\boldsymbol{x}}_{i},{\boldsymbol{F}}_{2}{\boldsymbol{x}}_{i}\rangle\Big|+C\,d^{-3/2}.

Here the inequality
(a)
follows since
|\mathbb{E}\{h^{\prime}(Z)-h^{\prime}(0)\}|\leq C\mathbb{E}\{Z^{2}\}
by smoothness of
h
and Taylor expansion,

\max_{i\leq n}|\langle{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0},{\boldsymbol{x}}_{i}\rangle|\leq C\sqrt{\log n}
by sub-Gaussian tail bounds, and we used Eq. (
195
)
for the last term.

The proof of
Eq. (
180
) is completed by showing that, with very high probability,
\max_{i\leq n}|\langle{\boldsymbol{x}}_{i},{\boldsymbol{F}}_{2}{\boldsymbol{x}}_{i}\rangle|\leq C\|{\mathsf{P}}_{>1}f^{*}\|_{L^{2}}\sqrt{d\log d}
. Without loss of generality, we assume here
\|{\mathsf{P}}_{>1}f^{*}\|_{L^{2}}=1
. In order to
show this claim, note that (defining
{\mathsf{P}}_{>0}f^{*}({\boldsymbol{x}}):=f^{*}({\boldsymbol{x}})-\mathbb{E}f^{*}({\boldsymbol{x}})
)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\langle{\boldsymbol{x}}_{i},{\boldsymbol{F}}_{2}{\boldsymbol{x}}_{i}\rangle=\mathsf{tr}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}{\boldsymbol{F}}_{2})\leq C\mathbb{E}\{{\mathsf{P}}_{>0}f^{*}({\boldsymbol{x}})\|{\boldsymbol{x}}\|_{2}^{2}\}\leq\text{Var}(\|{\boldsymbol{x}}\|_{2}^{2})^{1/2}\leq C\sqrt{d}\,.

(196)

Further notice that

\displaystyle\|{\boldsymbol{F}}_{2}\|

\displaystyle=\max_{\|{\boldsymbol{v}}\|_{2}=1}|\langle{\boldsymbol{v}},{\boldsymbol{F}}_{2}{\boldsymbol{v}}\rangle|

(197)

\displaystyle=\max_{\|{\boldsymbol{v}}\|_{2}=1}\big|\mathbb{E}\big\{{\mathsf{P}}_{>0}f^{*}({\boldsymbol{x}})\langle{\boldsymbol{v}},{\boldsymbol{x}}\rangle^{2}\big\}\big|

(198)

\displaystyle\leq\max_{\|{\boldsymbol{v}}\|_{2}=1}\mathbb{E}\big\{\langle{\boldsymbol{v}},{\boldsymbol{x}}\rangle^{4}\big\}^{1/2}\leq C\,.

(199)

By the above and the Hanson-Wright inequality

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(\langle{\boldsymbol{x}}_{i},{\boldsymbol{F}}_{2}{\boldsymbol{x}}_{i}\rangle\geq C\sqrt{d}+t\big)

\displaystyle\leq 2\,\exp\Big(-c\Big(\frac{t^{2}}{\|{\boldsymbol{F}}_{2}\|_{F}^{2}}\wedge\frac{t}{\|{\boldsymbol{F}}_{2}\|}\Big)\Big)\leq 2\,e^{-c((t^{2}/d)\wedge t)}\,,

(200)

and similarly for the lower tail.
By taking a union bound over
i\leq n
, we obtain
\max_{i\leq n}|\langle{\boldsymbol{x}}_{i},{\boldsymbol{F}}_{2}{\boldsymbol{x}}_{i}\rangle|\leq C\sqrt{d\log d}
as claimed, thus
completing the proof of Eq. (
180
).

We next prove Eq. (
181
). We claim that this bound
holds for any realization in
{\mathcal{E}}_{1}\cap{\mathcal{E}}_{2}
. Therefore we can fix
without loss of generality
i=1
,
j=2
. We use Eq. (
194
) with
k=4
. Using Cauchy-Schwarz and
Eqs. (
194
), (
195
), we get

\displaystyle M_{12}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\ell_{1},\ell_{2}=0}^{4}\frac{1}{\ell_{1}!\ell_{2}!}h_{1,\ell_{1}}h_{2,\ell_{2}}M_{1,2}(\ell_{1},\ell_{2})+\Delta_{12}\,,

(201)

\displaystyle M_{1,2}(\ell_{1},\ell_{2})

\displaystyle:=\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{x}}}\left\{{\rm He}_{\ell_{1}}\Big(\frac{1}{\sqrt{dQ_{11}}}\langle{\boldsymbol{x}}_{1},{\boldsymbol{x}}\rangle\Big){\rm He}_{\ell_{2}}\Big(\frac{1}{\sqrt{dQ_{22}}}\langle{\boldsymbol{x}}_{2},{\boldsymbol{x}}\rangle\Big)\right\}\,,\;\;\;\;|\Delta_{12}|\leq Cd^{-5/2}\,.

(202)

Note that, by Eq. (
188
),
|h_{ik}|\leq Cd^{-k/2}
, and
M_{1,2}(\ell_{1},\ell_{2})

is bounded on the event
{\mathcal{E}}_{1}\cap{\mathcal{E}}_{2}
, by the sub-Gaussianity of
{\boldsymbol{z}}
.
Comparing with Eqs. (
177
), (
179
), we get

\displaystyle|M_{12}-M_{0,12}|

\displaystyle\leq\Big|\sum_{\ell_{1},\ell_{2}=0}^{4}\frac{1}{\ell_{1}!\ell_{2}!}h_{1,\ell_{1}}h_{2,\ell_{2}}M_{1,2}(\ell_{1},\ell_{2})-M_{0,12}\Big|+Cd^{-5/2}

(203)

\displaystyle\phantom{AAAAA}+2\sum_{(\ell_{1},\ell_{2})\in{\mathcal{S}}}\big|h_{1,\ell_{1}}h_{2,\ell_{2}}M_{1,2}(\ell_{1},\ell_{2})\big|

(204)

\displaystyle\phantom{AAAAA}+2\Big|\frac{1}{6}h_{1,0}h_{2,3}M_{1,2}(0,3)-a_{1,0}a_{2,1}\Big|+|a_{1,1}a_{2,1}|+Cd^{-5/2}\,,

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}

\displaystyle:=\big\{(0,1),(0,2),(0,4),(1,2),(1,3),(2,2)\big\}\,,

(205)

where in the inequality we used the identities
h_{1,0}h_{2,0}M_{1,2}(0,0)=h_{1,0}h_{2,0}=a_{1,0}a_{2,0}
, and

\displaystyle h_{1,1}h_{2,1}M_{1,2}(1,1)=\frac{1}{d^{2}}\langle{\boldsymbol{x}}_{1},{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}{\boldsymbol{x}}_{2}\rangle\mathbb{E}h^{\prime}\Big(\sqrt{\frac{Q_{11}}{d}}G\Big)\mathbb{E}h^{\prime}\Big(\sqrt{\frac{Q_{22}}{d}}G\Big)=B_{12}\,.

We next bound each of the terms above separately.

We begin with the terms
(\ell_{1},\ell_{2})\in{\mathcal{S}}
. Since by Eq. (
188
),
|h_{ik}|\leq Cd^{-k/2}
,
for each of these pairs, we need to show
|M_{1,2}(\ell_{1},\ell_{2})|\leq Cd^{(\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}-5)/2}\log d
.
Consider
(\ell_{1},\ell_{2})=(0,k)
,
k\in\{1,2,4\}
. Set
{\boldsymbol{w}}={\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{1/2}{\boldsymbol{x}}_{2}/\sqrt{dQ_{22}}
,
\|{\boldsymbol{w}}\|_{2}=1
,
and write
{\rm He}_{k}(x)=\sum_{m=0}^{k}c_{k,\ell}x^{\ell}
. If
{\boldsymbol{g}}
is a standard Gaussian vector, we have
\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{g}}}{\rm He}_{k}\big(\langle{\boldsymbol{w}},{\boldsymbol{g}}\rangle)=0

and therefore

\displaystyle M_{1,2}(0,k)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{z}}}\left\{{\rm He}_{k}\big(\langle{\boldsymbol{w}},{\boldsymbol{z}}\rangle\big)\right\}-\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{g}}}\left\{{\rm He}_{k}\big(\langle{\boldsymbol{w}},{\boldsymbol{g}}\rangle\big)\right\}

(206)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\ell=0}^{k}c_{k,\ell}\sum_{i_{1},\dots,i_{\ell}\leq n}w_{i_{1}}\cdots w_{i_{\ell}}\big\{\mathbb{E}(z_{i_{1}}\cdots z_{i_{\ell}})-\mathbb{E}(g_{i_{1}}\cdots g_{i_{\ell}})\big\}\,.

(207)

Note that the only non-vanishing terms in the above sum are those in which all of the indices appearing in
(i_{1},\dots,i_{\ell})

appear at least twice, and at least one of the indices appears at least
3
times (because otherwise the two expectations are equal).
This immediately implies
M_{1,2}(0,1)=M_{1,2}(0,2)=0
. Analogously, all terms
\ell\leq 2
vanish in the above sum.

As for
k=4
, we have (recalling
{\rm He}_{4}(x)=x^{4}-3x^{2}
):

\displaystyle M_{1,2}(0,4)

\displaystyle=\left|\sum_{i_{1},\dots,i_{4}\leq n}w_{i_{1}}\cdots w_{i_{4}}\big\{\mathbb{E}(z_{i_{1}}\cdots z_{i_{4}})-\mathbb{E}(g_{i_{1}}\cdots g_{i_{4}})\big\}\right|

(208)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i\leq n}w_{i}^{4}\big|\mathbb{E}(z_{i}^{4})-3\big|\leq C\|{\boldsymbol{w}}\|^{2}_{\infty}\|{\boldsymbol{w}}\|_{2}^{2}\leq\frac{C\log d}{d}\,,

(209)

where the last inequality follows since
\|{\boldsymbol{w}}\|_{2}=1
by construction and
\|{\boldsymbol{w}}\|_{\infty}\leq C\sqrt{(\log d)/d}
on

{\mathcal{E}}_{1}\cap{\mathcal{E}}_{2}
.

Next consider
(\ell_{1},\ell_{2})=(1,2)
. Setting
{\boldsymbol{w}}_{i}={\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{1/2}{\boldsymbol{x}}_{i}/\sqrt{dQ_{ii}}
,
i\in\{1,2\}
, we get

\displaystyle M_{1,2}(1,2)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{z}}}\left\{{\rm He}_{1}\big(\langle{\boldsymbol{w}}_{1},{\boldsymbol{z}}\rangle\big){\rm He}_{2}\big(\langle{\boldsymbol{w}}_{2},{\boldsymbol{z}}\rangle\big)\right\}-\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{g}}}\left\{{\rm He}_{1}\big(\langle{\boldsymbol{w}}_{1},{\boldsymbol{g}}\rangle\big){\rm He}_{2}\big(\langle{\boldsymbol{w}}_{2},{\boldsymbol{g}}\rangle\big)\right\}

(210)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{z}}}\left\{\big(\langle{\boldsymbol{w}}_{1},{\boldsymbol{z}}\rangle\big)\big(\langle{\boldsymbol{w}}_{2},{\boldsymbol{z}}\rangle\big)^{2}\right\}-\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{g}}}\left\{\big(\langle{\boldsymbol{w}}_{1},{\boldsymbol{g}}\rangle\big)\big(\langle{\boldsymbol{w}}_{2},{\boldsymbol{g}}\rangle\big)^{2}\right\}

(211)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i_{1},i_{2},i_{3}\leq n}w_{1,i_{1}}w_{2,i_{2}}w_{2,i_{3}}\big\{\mathbb{E}(z_{i_{1}}z_{i_{2}}z_{i_{2}})-\mathbb{E}(g_{i_{1}}g_{i_{2}}g_{i_{3}})\big\}

(212)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{n}w_{1,i}w_{2,i}w_{2,i}\mathbb{E}(z_{i}^{3})\,.

(213)

Therefore, on
{\mathcal{E}}_{1}\cap{\mathcal{E}}_{2}
,

\displaystyle\big|M_{1,2}(1,2)\big|

\displaystyle\leq C\Big|\sum_{i=1}^{n}w_{1,i}w_{2,i}^{2}\Big|\leq\frac{C\log d}{d}\,.

(214)

Next consider
(\ell_{1},\ell_{2})=(1,3)
. Proceeding as above (and noting that the degree-one term in
{\rm He}_{3}
does not contribute),
we get

\displaystyle M_{1,2}(1,3)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{z}}}\left\{{\rm He}_{1}\big(\langle{\boldsymbol{w}}_{1},{\boldsymbol{z}}\rangle\big){\rm He}_{3}\big(\langle{\boldsymbol{w}}_{2},{\boldsymbol{z}}\rangle\big)\right\}-\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{g}}}\left\{{\rm He}_{1}\big(\langle{\boldsymbol{w}}_{1},{\boldsymbol{g}}\rangle\big){\rm He}_{3}\big(\langle{\boldsymbol{w}}_{2},{\boldsymbol{g}}\rangle\big)\right\}

(215)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{z}}}\left\{\big(\langle{\boldsymbol{w}}_{1},{\boldsymbol{z}}\rangle\big)\big(\langle{\boldsymbol{w}}_{2},{\boldsymbol{z}}\rangle\big)^{3}\right\}-\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{g}}}\left\{\big(\langle{\boldsymbol{w}}_{1},{\boldsymbol{g}}\rangle\big)\big(\langle{\boldsymbol{w}}_{2},{\boldsymbol{g}}\rangle\big)^{3}\right\}

(216)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i_{1},\dots,i_{4}\leq d}w_{1,i_{1}}w_{2,i_{2}}w_{2,i_{3}}w_{2,i_{4}}\big\{\mathbb{E}(z_{i_{1}}z_{i_{2}}z_{i_{2}}z_{i_{4}})-\mathbb{E}(g_{i_{1}}g_{i_{2}}g_{i_{3}}g_{i_{4}})\big\}

(217)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{d}w_{1,i}w_{2,i}^{3}(\mathbb{E}(z_{i}^{4})-3)\,.

(218)

Therefore, on
{\mathcal{E}}_{1}\cap{\mathcal{E}}_{2}

\displaystyle\big|M_{1,2}(1,3)\big|

\displaystyle\leq C\Big|\sum_{i=1}^{d}w_{1,i}w_{2,i}^{3}\Big|\leq C\|{\boldsymbol{w}}_{1}\|_{\infty}\|{\boldsymbol{w}}_{2}\|_{\infty}\|{\boldsymbol{w}}_{2}\|_{2}^{2}\leq\frac{C\log d}{d}\,.

(219)

Finally, for
(\ell_{1},\ell_{2})=(2,2)
, proceeding as above we get

\displaystyle M_{1,2}(2,2)

\displaystyle=\left|\sum_{i=1}^{d}w_{1,i}^{2}w_{2,i}^{2}(\mathbb{E}(z_{i}^{4})-3)\right|\leq C\|{\boldsymbol{w}}_{1}\|_{\infty}^{2}\|{\boldsymbol{w}}_{2}\|_{2}^{2}\leq\frac{C\log d}{d}\,.

(220)

Next consider the term
|h_{1,0}h_{2,3}M_{1,2}(0,3)/6-a_{1,0}a_{2,1}|
in Eq. (
204
). Using the fact that

h_{1,0}=a_{1,0}
is bounded, we get

\displaystyle\Big|\frac{1}{6}h_{1,0}h_{2,3}M_{1,2}(0,3)-a_{1,0}a_{2,1}\Big|\leq C\big|h_{2,3}M_{1,2}(0,3)-6a_{2,1}\big|\,.

(221)

Recalling
{\rm He}_{3}(x)=x^{3}-3x
, and letting
{\boldsymbol{w}}={\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{1/2}{\boldsymbol{x}}_{2}/\|{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{1/2}{\boldsymbol{x}}_{2}\|_{2}
:

\displaystyle M_{1,2}(0,3)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i_{1},\dots,i_{3}\leq d}w_{i_{1}}w_{i_{2}}w_{i_{3}}\big\{\mathbb{E}(z_{i_{1}}z_{i_{2}}z_{i_{3}})-\mathbb{E}(g_{i_{1}}g_{i_{2}}g_{i_{3}})\big\}

(222)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i\leq d}w_{i}^{3}\mathbb{E}(z_{i}^{3})\,.

(223)

In particular, on the event
{\mathcal{E}}_{1}\cap{\mathcal{E}}_{2}
,
|M_{1,2}(0,3)|\leq C\sqrt{(\log d)/d}
.
Comparing the definitions of
a_{2,1}
and
h_{2,3}
, we get

\displaystyle\big|h_{1,0}h_{2,3}M_{1,2}(0,3)-a_{1,0}a_{2,1}\big|

\displaystyle\leq C|M_{1,2}(0,3)|\times\left(\frac{Q_{22}}{d}\right)^{3/2}\Big|\mathbb{E}\Big\{h^{(3)}\Big(\sqrt{\frac{Q_{ii}}{d}}\,G\Big)\Big\}-h^{(3)}(0)\Big|

(224)

\displaystyle\leq C\sqrt{\frac{\log d}{d}}\times\frac{1}{d^{3/2}}\times\frac{1}{d^{1/2}}\leq\frac{C(\log d)^{1/2}}{d^{5/2}}\,.

(225)

Finally, consider term
|a_{1,1}a_{2,1}|
in Eq. (
204
). By the above estimates, we get
|a_{2,1}|\leq Cd^{-2}(\log d)^{1/2}
,
and hence this term is negligible as well. This completes the proof of Eq. (
181
).

Equation (
182
) follows by a similar argument, which we omit.
∎

A.2.2
An estimate on the entries of the resolvent

Lemma A.4
.

Let
{\boldsymbol{Z}}=(z_{ij})_{i\leq n,j\leq d}
be a random matrix with
iid rows
{\boldsymbol{z}}_{1},\dots,{\boldsymbol{z}}_{n}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
that are zero mean and
C
-sub-Gaussian.
Further assume
C^{-1}\leq n/d\leq C
. Let
{\boldsymbol{S}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d\times d}
be a symmetric
matrix such that
{\boldsymbol{0}}\preceq{\boldsymbol{S}}\preceq C{\mathbf{I}}_{d}
for some finite constant
C>1
.
Finally, let
g:{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\to{\mathbb{R}}
be a measurable function such that
\mathbb{E}\{g({\boldsymbol{z}}_{1})\}=\mathbb{E}\{{\boldsymbol{z}}_{1}g({\boldsymbol{z}}_{1})\}=0
,
and
\mathbb{E}\{g({\boldsymbol{z}}_{1})^{2}\}=1
.

Then, for any
\lambda>0
there exists a finite constant
C
such that, for any
i\neq j
,

\displaystyle\Big|\mathbb{E}\Big\{\big({\boldsymbol{Z}}{\boldsymbol{S}}{\boldsymbol{Z}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{\,T}}}/d+\lambda{\mathbf{I}}_{n}\big)^{-1}_{i,j}g({\boldsymbol{z}}_{i})g({\boldsymbol{z}}_{j})\Big\}\Big|\leq C\,d^{-3/2}\,.

(226)

Proof.

Without loss of generality, we can consider
i=1
,
j=2
. Further, we let
{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{(n-2)\times d}

be the matrix comprising the last
n-2
rows of
{\boldsymbol{Z}}
, and
{\boldsymbol{U}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d\times 2}
be the matrix with columns

{\boldsymbol{U}}{\boldsymbol{e}}_{1}={\boldsymbol{z}}_{1}
,
{\boldsymbol{U}}{\boldsymbol{e}}_{2}={\boldsymbol{z}}_{2}
. We finally define the matrices
{\boldsymbol{R}}_{0}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d\times d}
and

{\boldsymbol{Y}}=(Y_{ij})_{i,j\leq 2}
:

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{R}}_{0}

\displaystyle:=\lambda{\boldsymbol{S}}^{1/2}\big({\boldsymbol{S}}^{1/2}{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{\,T}}}{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0}{\boldsymbol{S}}^{1/2}/d+\lambda{\mathbf{I}}_{d}\big)^{-1}{\boldsymbol{S}}^{1/2}\,,

(227)

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{Y}}

\displaystyle:=\big({\boldsymbol{Z}}{\boldsymbol{S}}{\boldsymbol{Z}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{\,T}}}/d+\lambda{\mathbf{I}}_{n}\big)^{-1}\,.

(228)

Then, by a simple linear algebra calculation, we have

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{Y}}

\displaystyle=\Big({\boldsymbol{U}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{\,T}}}{\boldsymbol{R}}_{0}{\boldsymbol{U}}/d+\lambda{\mathbf{I}}_{2}\Big)^{-1}\,,

(229)

\displaystyle Y_{12}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\langle{\boldsymbol{z}}_{1},{\boldsymbol{R}}_{0}{\boldsymbol{z}}_{2}\rangle/d}{(\lambda+\langle{\boldsymbol{z}}_{1},{\boldsymbol{R}}_{0}{\boldsymbol{z}}_{2}\rangle/d)(\lambda+\langle{\boldsymbol{z}}_{1},{\boldsymbol{R}}_{0}{\boldsymbol{z}}_{2}\rangle/d)-\langle{\boldsymbol{z}}_{1},{\boldsymbol{R}}_{0}{\boldsymbol{z}}_{2}\rangle^{2}/d^{2}}\,.

(230)

Note that since
{\boldsymbol{R}}_{0}\succeq 0
, we have
\langle{\boldsymbol{z}}_{1},{\boldsymbol{R}}_{0}{\boldsymbol{z}}_{2}\rangle^{2}\leq\langle{\boldsymbol{z}}_{1},{\boldsymbol{R}}_{0}{\boldsymbol{z}}_{1}\rangle\langle{\boldsymbol{z}}_{2},{\boldsymbol{R}}_{0}{\boldsymbol{z}}_{2}\rangle
, and therefore

\displaystyle Y_{12}

\displaystyle=Y_{12}^{(1)}+Y_{12}^{(2)}\,,

(231)

\displaystyle Y_{12}^{(1)}

\displaystyle:=-\frac{\langle{\boldsymbol{z}}_{1},{\boldsymbol{R}}_{0}{\boldsymbol{z}}_{2}\rangle/d}{(\lambda+\langle{\boldsymbol{z}}_{1},{\boldsymbol{R}}_{0}{\boldsymbol{z}}_{1}\rangle/d)(\lambda+\langle{\boldsymbol{z}}_{2},{\boldsymbol{R}}_{0}{\boldsymbol{z}}_{2}\rangle/d)}\,,

(232)

\displaystyle|Y_{12}^{(2)}|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\lambda^{4}d^{3}}|\langle{\boldsymbol{z}}_{1},{\boldsymbol{R}}_{0}{\boldsymbol{z}}_{2}\rangle|^{3}\,.

(233)

Denote by
\mathbb{E}_{+}
expectation with respect to
{\boldsymbol{z}}_{1},{\boldsymbol{z}}_{2}
(conditional on
({\boldsymbol{z}}_{i})_{2<i\leq n}
).
We have

\displaystyle\big|\mathbb{E}_{+}\{Y_{12}\,g({\boldsymbol{z}}_{1})\,g({\boldsymbol{z}}_{2})\}\big|

\displaystyle\leq\big|\mathbb{E}_{+}\{Y_{12}^{(1)}\,g({\boldsymbol{z}}_{1})\,g({\boldsymbol{z}}_{2})\}\big|+\mathbb{E}_{+}\{(Y_{12}^{(2)})^{2}\}^{1/2}\,\mathbb{E}_{+}\{g({\boldsymbol{z}}_{1})^{2}\,g({\boldsymbol{z}}_{2})^{2}\}^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\big|\mathbb{E}_{+}\{Y_{12}^{(1)}\,g({\boldsymbol{z}}_{1})\,g({\boldsymbol{z}}_{2})\}\big|+\mathbb{E}_{+}\{(Y_{12}^{(2)})^{2}\}^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\big|\mathbb{E}_{+}\{Y_{12}^{(1)}\,g({\boldsymbol{z}}_{1})\,g({\boldsymbol{z}}_{2})\}\big|+C\,d^{-3/2}\,.

Here the last step follows by the Hanson-Wright inequality.
We therefore only have to bound the first term. Defining
q_{j}:=\lambda+\langle{\boldsymbol{z}}_{j},{\boldsymbol{R}}_{0}{\boldsymbol{z}}_{j}\rangle/d
,

\overline{q}_{j}=\mathbb{E}_{+}q_{j}
,
g_{j}=g({\boldsymbol{z}}_{j})
,
j\in\{1,2\}
,

\displaystyle\big|\mathbb{E}_{+}\{Y_{12}^{(1)}\,g_{1}\,g_{2}\}\big|

\displaystyle\leq\Big|\mathbb{E}_{+}\Big\{\overline{q}^{-2}\frac{\langle{\boldsymbol{z}}_{1},{\boldsymbol{R}}_{0}{\boldsymbol{z}}_{2}\rangle}{d}g_{1}g_{2}\Big\}\Big|

\displaystyle\qquad{}+2\Big|\mathbb{E}_{+}\Big\{\Big(q_{1}^{-1}-\overline{q}^{-1}\Big)\overline{q}^{-2}\frac{\langle{\boldsymbol{z}}_{1},{\boldsymbol{R}}_{0}{\boldsymbol{z}}_{2}\rangle}{d}g_{1}g_{2}\Big\}\Big|

\displaystyle\qquad{}+\Big|\mathbb{E}\Big\{\Big(q_{1}^{-1}-\overline{q}^{-1}\Big)\Big(q_{2}^{-1}-\overline{q}^{-1}\Big)\frac{\langle{\boldsymbol{z}}_{1},{\boldsymbol{R}}_{0}{\boldsymbol{z}}_{2}\rangle}{d}g_{1}g_{2}\Big\}\Big|

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{\leq}}\Big|\mathbb{E}_{+}\Big\{\Big(q_{1}^{-1}-\overline{q}^{-1}\Big)\Big(q_{2}^{-1}-\overline{q}^{-1}\Big)\frac{\langle{\boldsymbol{z}}_{1},{\boldsymbol{R}}_{0}{\boldsymbol{z}}_{2}\rangle}{d}g_{1}g_{2}\Big\}\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\lambda^{4}}\mathbb{E}\Big\{|q_{1}-\overline{q}||q_{2}-\overline{q}|\Big|\frac{\langle{\boldsymbol{z}}_{1},{\boldsymbol{R}}_{0}{\boldsymbol{z}}_{2}\rangle}{d}\Big||g_{1}g_{2}|\Big\}\,.

Here
(a)
follows from the orthogonality of
g({\boldsymbol{z}})
to linear functions.

We then conclude

\displaystyle\big|\mathbb{E}_{+}\{Y^{(1)}_{12}\,g_{1}\,g_{2}\}\big|

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{\leq}}C\mathbb{E}_{+}\big\{|q_{1}-\overline{q}|^{8}\big\}^{1/4}\mathbb{E}_{+}\big\{(\langle{\boldsymbol{z}}_{1},{\boldsymbol{R}}_{0}{\boldsymbol{z}}_{2}\rangle/d)^{4}\big\}^{1/4}

\displaystyle\leq C\mathbb{E}_{+}\big\{|\langle{\boldsymbol{z}}_{1},{\boldsymbol{R}}_{0}{\boldsymbol{z}}_{1}\rangle/d-\mathbb{E}_{+}\langle{\boldsymbol{z}}_{1},{\boldsymbol{R}}_{0}{\boldsymbol{z}}_{1}\rangle/d|^{8}\big\}^{1/4}\mathbb{E}_{+}\big\{(\langle{\boldsymbol{z}}_{1},{\boldsymbol{R}}_{0}{\boldsymbol{z}}_{2}\rangle/d)^{4}\big\}^{1/4}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(b)}}{{\leq}}C(d^{-1/2})^{2}\times Cd^{-1/2}\leq Cd^{-3/2}\,.

Here
(a)
follows from Hölder’s inequality and
(b)
from
the Hanson-Wright inequality using the fact that
\|{\boldsymbol{R}}_{0}\|
is bounded. The proof is completed by taking expectation over
({\boldsymbol{z}}_{i})_{2<i\leq n}
.
∎

Lemma A.5
.

Under the definitions and assumptions of
Lemma
A.4
, let

Y_{ij}:=({\boldsymbol{Z}}{\boldsymbol{S}}{\boldsymbol{Z}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{\,T}}}/d+\lambda{\mathbf{I}}_{n})^{-1}_{i,j}
.
Then, for any tuple of four distinct indices

i,j,k,l
, we have

\displaystyle\big|\mathbb{E}\{Y_{ij}Y_{kl}g({\boldsymbol{z}}_{i})g({\boldsymbol{z}}_{j})g({\boldsymbol{z}}_{k})g({\boldsymbol{z}}_{l})\}\big|\leq Cd^{-5/2}\,.

(234)

Proof.

The proof is analogous to the one of Lemma
A.4
. Without loss of generality,
we set
(i,j,k,l)=(1,2,3,4)
, denote by
{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{(n-4)\times d}
the matrix with rows
({\boldsymbol{z}}_{\ell})_{\ell\geq 5}
, and define the
d\times d
matrix

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{R}}_{0}

\displaystyle:=\lambda{\boldsymbol{S}}^{1/2}\big({\boldsymbol{S}}^{1/2}{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{\,T}}}{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0}{\boldsymbol{S}}^{1/2}/d+\lambda{\mathbf{I}}_{n-2}\big)^{-1}{\boldsymbol{S}}^{1/2}\,.

(235)

We then have that
{\boldsymbol{Y}}=(Y_{ij})_{i,j\leq 4}
is given by

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{Y}}

\displaystyle=(\text{diag}({\boldsymbol{q}})+{\boldsymbol{A}})^{-1}\,,

(236)

\displaystyle q_{i}

\displaystyle:=\overline{q}+Q_{i}\,,\;\;\;\overline{q}:=\lambda+\mathsf{tr}({\boldsymbol{R}}_{0})/d\,,\;\;Q_{i}=(\langle{\boldsymbol{z}}_{i},{\boldsymbol{R}}_{0}{\boldsymbol{z}}_{i}\rangle-\mathbb{E}\langle{\boldsymbol{z}}_{i},{\boldsymbol{R}}_{0}{\boldsymbol{z}}_{i}\rangle)/d\,,

(237)

\displaystyle A_{ij}

\displaystyle:=\begin{cases}\langle{\boldsymbol{z}}_{i},{\boldsymbol{R}}_{0}{\boldsymbol{z}}_{j}\rangle/d&\mbox{ if $i\neq j$,}\\
0&\mbox{ if $i=j$.}\\
\end{cases}

(238)

In what follows we denote by
\mathbb{E}_{+}
expectation with respect
to
({\boldsymbol{z}}_{i})_{i\leq 4}
, with
{\boldsymbol{Z}}_{0}
fixed.
Note that, by the Hanson-Wright inequality,

\mathbb{E}_{+}\{|A_{ij}|^{k}\}^{1/k}\leq c_{k}\,d^{-1/2}
,

\mathbb{E}_{+}\{|Q_{i}|^{k}\}^{1/k}\leq c_{k}\,d^{-1/2}
for each
k\geq 1
.
We next compute the Taylor expansion of
Y_{12}
and
Y_{3,4}
in powers of
{\boldsymbol{A}}
to get

\displaystyle Y_{12}

\displaystyle=Y^{(1)}_{12}+Y^{(2)}_{12}+Y^{(3)}_{12}+Y^{(4)}_{12}\,,

(239)

\displaystyle Y^{(1)}_{12}

\displaystyle:=-q_{1}^{-1}A_{12}q_{2}\,,

(240)

\displaystyle Y^{(2)}_{12}

\displaystyle:=q_{1}^{-1}A_{13}q_{3}^{-1}A_{32}q_{2}^{-1}+q_{1}^{-1}A_{14}q_{4}^{-1}A_{41}q_{2}^{-1}\,,

(241)

\displaystyle Y^{(3)}_{12}

\displaystyle:=-\sum_{i_{1}\neq i_{2},i_{1}\neq 1i_{2}\neq 2}q_{1}^{-1}A_{1i_{1}}q_{i_{1}}^{-1}A_{i_{1}i_{2}}q_{i_{2}}^{-1}A_{i_{2}2}q_{2}^{-1}\,,

(242)

and similarly for
Y_{34}
.
It is easy to show that
\mathbb{E}_{+}\{|Y^{(\ell)}_{ab}|^{k}\}^{1/k}\leq c_{k}d^{-\ell/2}
, for all
k\geq 1
. Therefore,
using
\mathbb{E}\{g({\boldsymbol{z}}_{i})^{2}\}\leq C
and Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, and writing
g_{i}=g({\boldsymbol{z}}_{i})
:

\displaystyle\big|\mathbb{E}\{Y_{12}Y_{34}g_{1}g_{2}g_{3}g_{4}\}\big|=\sum_{\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}\leq 4}\big|\mathbb{E}\{Y^{(\ell_{1})}_{12}Y^{(\ell_{2})}_{34}g_{1}g_{2}g_{3}g_{4}\}\big|+Cd^{-5/2}\,.

(243)

The proof is completed by bounding each of the terms above, which we now do. By symmetry
it is sufficient to consider
\ell_{1}\leq\ell_{2}
and therefore we are left with the
4
pairs

(\ell_{1},\ell_{2})\in\{(1,1),(1,2),(1,3),(2,2)\}
.

Term
(\ell_{1},\ell_{2})=(1,1)
.
By the same argument as in the proof of Lemma
A.4
, we have
|\mathbb{E}\{A_{ij}q_{i}^{-1}q_{j}^{-1}g_{i}g_{j}\}|\leq Cd^{-3/2}
and therefore

\displaystyle\big|\mathbb{E}_{+}\{Y^{(1)}_{12}Y^{(1)}_{34}g_{1}g_{2}g_{3}g_{4}\}\big|=\big|\mathbb{E}_{+}\{A_{12}q_{1}^{-1}q_{2}^{-1}g_{1}g_{2}\}\big|\cdot\big|\mathbb{E}\{A_{34}q_{3}^{-1}q_{4}^{-1}g_{3}g_{4}\}\big|\leq Cd^{-3}\,.

(244)

Term
(\ell_{1},\ell_{2})=(1,2)
.
Note that each of the two terms in the definition of

Y^{(2)}_{34}
contributes a summand with the same
structure. Hence we can consider just the one
resulting in the largest expectation, say
q_{3}^{-1}A_{31}q_{1}^{-1}A_{14}q_{4}^{-1}

\displaystyle\big|\mathbb{E}_{+}\{Y^{(1)}_{12}Y^{(2)}_{34}g_{1}g_{2}g_{3}g_{4}\}\big|

\displaystyle=2\big|\mathbb{E}_{+}\{q_{1}^{-1}A_{12}q^{-1}_{2}q_{3}^{-1}A_{31}q_{1}^{-1}A_{14}q_{4}^{-1}g_{1}g_{2}g_{3}g_{4}\}\big|

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{=}}2\big|\mathbb{E}_{+}\{q_{1}^{-2}A_{12}(q^{-1}_{2}-\overline{q}^{-1})(q_{3}^{-1}-\overline{q}^{-1})A_{31}A_{14}(q_{4}^{-1}-\overline{q}^{-1})g_{1}g_{2}g_{3}g_{4}\}\big|

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(b)}}{{\leq}}C\mathbb{E}_{+}\{|A_{12}|^{p}\}^{1/p}\mathbb{E}\{|A_{13}|^{p}\}^{1/p}\mathbb{E}\{|A_{13}|^{p}\}^{1/p}\mathbb{E}_{+}\{|q^{-1}_{2}-\overline{q}^{-1}|^{p}\}^{1/p}\mathbb{E}\{|q^{-1}_{3}-\overline{q}^{-1}|^{p}\}^{1/p}

\displaystyle\phantom{AAAAA}\cdot\mathbb{E}\{|q^{-1}_{4}-\overline{q}^{-1}|^{p}\}^{1/p}\|g\|^{4}_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(c)}}{{\leq}}Cd^{-3}\,.

Here
(a)
holds because
g_{i}
is orthogonal to
{\boldsymbol{z}}_{i}
for
i\in\{2,3,4\}
and hence the terms
\overline{q}^{-1}

have vanishing contribution;
(b)
by Hölder for
p=12
, and using the fact that
q_{i}^{-1}
is bounded;
(c)
by the above bounds on the moments of
A_{ij}
,
Q_{i}
, plus

|q^{-1}_{i}-\overline{q}^{-1}|\leq C|Q_{i}|
.

Term
(\ell_{1},\ell_{2})=(1,3)
.
Taking into account symmetries,
there are only two distinct terms to consider in the sum
defining
Y^{(3)}_{34}
, which we can identify with the
following ones:

\displaystyle\big|\mathbb{E}_{+}\{Y^{(1)}_{12}Y^{(3)}_{34}

\displaystyle g_{1}g_{2}g_{3}g_{4}\}\big|\leq C\big|\mathbb{E}_{+}\big\{q_{1}^{-1}A_{12}q^{-1}_{2}q_{3}^{-1}A_{31}q_{1}^{-1}A_{12}q_{2}^{-1}A_{24}q_{4}^{-1}g_{1}g_{2}g_{3}g_{4}\big\}\big|

\displaystyle+C\big|\mathbb{E}_{+}\big\{q_{1}^{-1}A_{12}q^{-1}_{2}q_{3}^{-1}A_{31}q_{1}^{-1}A_{13}q_{3}^{-1}A_{34}q_{4}^{-1}g_{1}g_{2}g_{3}g_{4}\big\}\big|=:C\cdot T_{1}+C\cdot T_{2}\,.

Notice that in the first term
{\boldsymbol{z}}_{3}
only appears in
q_{3}
,
A_{31}
, and
g_{3}
, and similarly
{\boldsymbol{z}}_{4}
only appears in
q_{4}
,
A_{24}
, and
g_{4}
. Hence

\displaystyle T_{1}=\big|\mathbb{E}_{+}\big\{q_{1}^{-1}A_{12}q^{-1}_{2}(q_{3}^{-1}-\overline{q}^{-1})A_{31}q_{1}^{-1}A_{12}q_{2}^{-1}A_{24}(q_{4}^{-1}-\overline{q}^{-1})g_{1}g_{2}g_{3}g_{4}\big\}\big|\leq Cd^{-3}\,,

where the last inequality follows again by Hölder.
Analogously, for the second term we have

\displaystyle T_{2}=\big|\mathbb{E}_{+}\big\{q_{1}^{-1}A_{12}(q^{-1}_{2}-\overline{q}^{-1})q_{3}^{-1}A_{31}q_{1}^{-1}A_{32}q_{3}^{-1}A_{24}(q_{4}^{-1}-\overline{q}^{-1})g_{1}g_{2}g_{3}g_{4}\big\}\big|\leq Cd^{-3}\,,

This proves the desired bound for
(\ell_{1},\ell_{2})=(1,3)
.

Term
(\ell_{1},\ell_{2})=(2,2)
.
There are four terms that arise from the sum in the
definition of
Y^{(2)}_{ij}
. By symmetry, these are equivalent by pairs

\displaystyle\big|\mathbb{E}_{+}\{Y^{(2)}_{12}Y^{(2)}_{34}g_{1}g_{2}g_{3}g_{4}\}\big|

\displaystyle\leq 2\big|\mathbb{E}_{+}\big\{q_{1}^{-1}A_{13}q^{-1}_{3}A_{32}q_{2}^{-1}q_{3}^{-1}A_{31}q_{1}^{-1}A_{14}q_{4}^{-1}g_{1}g_{2}g_{3}g_{4}\big\}\big|

\displaystyle\qquad{}+2\big|\mathbb{E}_{+}\big\{q_{1}^{-1}A_{13}q^{-1}_{3}A_{32}q_{2}^{-1}q_{3}^{-1}A_{32}q_{2}^{-1}A_{24}q_{4}^{-1}g_{1}g_{2}g_{3}g_{4}\big\}\big|

\displaystyle\leq 2\big|\mathbb{E}_{+}\big\{q_{1}^{-1}A_{13}q^{-1}_{3}A_{32}(q_{2}^{-1}-\overline{q}^{-1})q_{3}^{-1}A_{31}q_{1}^{-1}A_{14}(q_{4}^{-1}-\overline{q}^{-1})g_{1}g_{2}g_{3}g_{4}\big\}\big|

\displaystyle\qquad{}+2\big|\mathbb{E}_{+}\big\{(q_{1}^{-1}-\overline{q}^{-1})A_{13}q^{-1}_{3}A_{32}q_{2}^{-1}q_{3}^{-1}A_{32}q_{2}^{-1}A_{24}(q_{4}^{-1}-\overline{q}^{-1})g_{1}g_{2}g_{3}g_{4}\big\}\big|

\displaystyle\leq Cd^{-3}\,.

This completes the proof of this lemma.
∎

Lemma A.6
.

Under the definitions and assumptions of Lemma
A.5
, further assume
\mathbb{E}\{|g({\boldsymbol{z}})|^{2+\eta}\}\leq C
for some constants
0<C,\eta<\infty
.
for any triple of four distinct indices

i,j,k
, we have

\displaystyle\big|\mathbb{E}\{Y_{ij}Y_{jk}g({\boldsymbol{z}}_{i})g({\boldsymbol{z}}_{j})^{2}g({\boldsymbol{z}}_{k})\}\big|

\displaystyle\leq Cd^{-3/2}\,,

(245)

\displaystyle\big|\mathbb{E}\{Y_{ij}^{2}g({\boldsymbol{z}}_{i})^{2}g({\boldsymbol{z}}_{l})^{2}\}\big|

\displaystyle\leq Cd^{-1}\,.

(246)

Proof.

This proof is very similar to the one of Lemma

A.5
, and we will follow the same
notation introduced there.

Consider Eq. (
245
). Without loss of generality, we take
(i,j,k)=(1,2,3)
.
Since
\mathbb{E}\{|Y^{(\ell)}_{ij}|^{k}\}\leq c_{k}d^{-\ell/2}
, we have

\displaystyle\big|\mathbb{E}_{+}\{Y_{12}Y_{23}g_{1}g_{2}^{2}g_{3}\}\big|\leq\big|\mathbb{E}_{+}\{Y_{12}^{(1)}Y^{(1)}_{23}g_{1}g_{2}^{2}g_{3}\}\big|+Cd^{-3/2}\,.

(247)

Further

\displaystyle\big|\mathbb{E}_{+}\{Y_{12}^{(1)}Y^{(1)}_{23}g_{1}g_{2}^{2}g_{3}\}\big|

\displaystyle=\big|\mathbb{E}_{+}\{q_{1}^{-1}A_{12}q_{2}^{-2}A_{23}q_{3}^{-1}g_{1}g_{2}^{2}g_{3}\}\big|

\displaystyle=\big|\mathbb{E}_{+}\{(q_{1}^{-1}-\overline{q}^{-1})A_{12}q_{2}^{-2}A_{23}(q_{3}^{-1}-\overline{q}^{-1})g_{1}g_{2}^{2}g_{3}\}\big|

\displaystyle\leq Cd^{-2}\,,

where the last bound follows from Hölder inequality.

Finally, Eq. (
246
) follows immediately by Hölder inequality since
\mathbb{E}\{|Y_{ij}|^{k}\}^{1/k}\leq C_{k}d^{-1/2}
for all
k
.
∎

Theorem A.7
.

Let
{\boldsymbol{Z}}=(z_{ij})_{i\leq n,j\leq d}
be a random matrix with iid rows
{\boldsymbol{z}}_{1},\dots,{\boldsymbol{z}}_{n}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
, with zero mean
C
-sub-Gaussian.
Let
{\boldsymbol{S}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d\times d}
be a symmetric
matrix such that
{\boldsymbol{0}}\preceq{\boldsymbol{S}}\preceq C{\mathbf{I}}_{d}
for some finite constant
C>1
.
Finally, let
g:{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\to{\mathbb{R}}
be a measurable function such that
\mathbb{E}\{g({\boldsymbol{z}}_{1})\}=\mathbb{E}\{{\boldsymbol{z}}_{1}g({\boldsymbol{z}}_{1})\}=0
,
and
\mathbb{E}\{|g({\boldsymbol{z}}_{1})|^{4+\eta}\}\leq C
.

Then, for any
\lambda>0
, with probability at least
1-Cd^{-1/4}
, we have

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{d}\sum_{i<j\leq n}\big({\boldsymbol{Z}}{\boldsymbol{S}}{\boldsymbol{Z}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{\,T}}}/d+\lambda{\mathbf{I}}_{n}\big)^{-1}_{i,j}g({\boldsymbol{z}}_{i})g({\boldsymbol{z}}_{j})\right|\leq C\,d^{-1/8}\,.

(248)

Proof.

Denote by
X
the sum on the left-hand side of Eq. (
248
), and define

Y_{ij}:=({\boldsymbol{Z}}{\boldsymbol{S}}{\boldsymbol{Z}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{\,T}}}/d+\lambda{\mathbf{I}}_{n})^{-1}_{i,j}
,
g_{i}=g({\boldsymbol{z}}_{i})
. Further,
let

{\mathcal{I}}_{m}:=\{(i,j,k,l):\;i<j\leq n,k<l\leq n,|\{i,j\}\cap\{k,j\}|=m\}
,
m\in\{0,1\}
. Then we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\{X^{2}\}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{d^{2}}\sum_{i<j}\sum_{k<l}\mathbb{E}\{Y_{ij}Y_{kl}g_{i}g_{j}g_{k}g_{l}\}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{d^{2}}\sum_{(i,j,k,l)\in{\mathcal{I}}_{0}}\mathbb{E}\{Y_{ij}Y_{kl}g_{i}g_{j}g_{k}g_{l}\}+\frac{1}{d^{2}}\sum_{(i,j,k,l)\in{\mathcal{I}}_{1}}\mathbb{E}\{Y_{ij}Y_{kl}g_{i}g_{j}g_{k}g_{l}\}++\frac{1}{d^{2}}\sum_{i<j}\mathbb{E}\{Y_{ij}^{2}g^{2}_{i}g_{j}^{2}\}

\displaystyle\leq Cd^{2}\big|\mathbb{E}\{Y_{12}Y_{34}g_{1}g_{2}g_{3}g_{4}\}\big|+Cd\big|\mathbb{E}\{Y_{12}Y_{23}g_{1}g^{2}_{2}g_{3}\}\big|+C\big|\mathbb{E}\{Y^{2}_{12}g^{2}_{1}g^{2}_{2}\}\big|

\displaystyle\leq Cd^{-1/2}\,.

The proof is completed by Chebyshev inequality.
∎

A.2.3
Proof of Theorem
4.13
: Variance term

Throughout this section we will refer to the events
{\mathcal{E}}_{1}
,
{\mathcal{E}}_{2}
defined in Eqs. (
183
), (
185
).
The variance is given by

\displaystyle{\widehat{\textsc{var}}}=\sigma_{\xi}^{2}\mathbb{E}_{{\boldsymbol{x}}}\big\{K({\boldsymbol{x}},{\boldsymbol{X}})^{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{\,T}}}K({\boldsymbol{X}},{\boldsymbol{X}})^{-2}K({\boldsymbol{x}},{\boldsymbol{X}})\big\}\,.

(249)

The following lemma allows us to take the expectation with respect to
{\boldsymbol{x}}
.

Lemma A.8
.

Under the assumptions of Theorem
4.13
, define
{\boldsymbol{M}}_{0}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n\times n}
as in the statement of
Lemma
A.3
.
Then, with very high probability, we have

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{\sigma_{\xi}^{2}}{\widehat{\textsc{var}}}-\langle{\boldsymbol{M}}_{0},{\boldsymbol{K}}^{-2}\rangle\right|\leq\frac{C\log d}{d}\,.

(250)

Proof.

First notice that, defining
{\boldsymbol{M}}
as in Eq. (
173
), we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sigma_{\xi}^{2}}{\widehat{\textsc{var}}}=\langle{\boldsymbol{M}},{\boldsymbol{K}}^{-2}\rangle\,.

(251)

We then have, with very high probability,

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{\sigma^{2}}{\widehat{\textsc{var}}}-\langle{\boldsymbol{M}}_{0},{\boldsymbol{K}}^{-2}\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left|\langle{\boldsymbol{M}}-{\boldsymbol{M}}_{0},{\boldsymbol{K}}^{-2}\rangle\right|

(252)

\displaystyle\leq\|{\boldsymbol{M}}-{\boldsymbol{M}}_{0}\|_{F}\sqrt{n}\|{\boldsymbol{K}}^{-2}\|

(253)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{\leq}}\frac{C\log d}{d^{3/2}}\times\sqrt{d}\times\|{\boldsymbol{K}}^{-1}\|^{2}

(254)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(b)}}{{\leq}}\frac{C\log d}{d}\,,

(255)

where
(a)
follows from Lemma
A.3
and
(b)
from Lemma
A.2
.
∎

In the following we define
{\boldsymbol{B}}_{0}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n\times n}
via

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{B}}_{0}:=\frac{h^{\prime}(0)}{d^{2}}{\boldsymbol{X}}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{\,T}}}\,.

(256)

The next lemma shows that
{\boldsymbol{B}}_{0}
is a good approximation for
{\boldsymbol{B}}
, defined in Eq. (
177
).

Lemma A.9
.

Let
{\boldsymbol{B}}
be defined as per Eq. (
177
).
With very high probability, we have
\|{\boldsymbol{B}}-{\boldsymbol{B}}_{0}\|\leq Cd^{-3/2}
and

\|{\boldsymbol{B}}-{\boldsymbol{B}}_{0}\|_{*}\leq Cd^{-1/2}
.

Proof.

Notice that
{\boldsymbol{B}}={\boldsymbol{D}}{\boldsymbol{X}}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{\,T}}}{\boldsymbol{D}}/d^{2}
and, on
{\mathcal{E}}_{1}\cap{\mathcal{E}}_{2}
,

\displaystyle\big\|{\boldsymbol{D}}-h^{\prime}(0){\mathbf{I}}\|=\max_{i\leq n}\Big|\mathbb{E}h^{\prime}\Big(\sqrt{\frac{Q_{ii}}{d}}G\Big)-h^{\prime}(0)\Big|\leq\frac{C}{\sqrt{d}}\,.

(257)

We then have

\displaystyle\big\|{\boldsymbol{B}}-{\boldsymbol{B}}_{0}\big\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{\sqrt{d}}\Big\|\frac{1}{d^{2}}{\boldsymbol{X}}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{\,T}}}\Big\|\leq\frac{C}{d^{5/2}}\|{\boldsymbol{X}}\|^{2}\leq\frac{C}{d^{3/2}}\,.

(258)

This immediately implies
\|{\boldsymbol{B}}-{\boldsymbol{B}}_{0}\|_{*}\leq n\|{\boldsymbol{B}}-{\boldsymbol{B}}_{0}\|\leq C/\sqrt{d}
.
∎

Lemma A.10
.

Under the assumptions of Theorem
4.13
, let
{\boldsymbol{B}}
be defined as per Eq. (
177
)and

{\boldsymbol{B}}_{0}
as per Eq. (
256
). Also, recall the definition of
{\boldsymbol{K}}_{1}
in Eq. (
163
). Then, with very high probability, we have

\displaystyle\big|\langle{\boldsymbol{B}},{\boldsymbol{K}}^{-2}\rangle-\langle{\boldsymbol{B}}_{0},{\boldsymbol{K}}_{1}^{-2}\rangle\big|\leq C\,n^{-c_{0}}\,.

(259)

Proof.

Throughout this proof, we work under events
{\mathcal{E}}_{1}\cap{\mathcal{E}}_{2}
defined in the proof of Lemma
A.3
.
Recall that
\max_{i\leq n}|D_{i}|
is bounded (see, e.g., Eq. (
257
)), whence

\displaystyle|B_{ij}|\leq\frac{C}{d^{2}}\big|\langle{\boldsymbol{x}}_{i},{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}{\boldsymbol{x}}_{j}\rangle\big|\leq\begin{cases}C/d&\mbox{ if $i=j$,}\\
C(\log d)^{1/2}/d^{3/2}&\mbox{ if $i\neq j$,}\end{cases}

(260)

whence
\|{\boldsymbol{B}}\|_{F}\leq C\,\sqrt{(\log d)/d}
. Using Lemma
A.2
, we have

\displaystyle\big|\langle{\boldsymbol{B}},{\boldsymbol{K}}^{-2}\rangle-\langle{\boldsymbol{B}},{\boldsymbol{K}}_{1}^{-2}\rangle\big|

\displaystyle\leq\|{\boldsymbol{B}}\|_{F}n^{1/2}\|{\boldsymbol{K}}^{-2}-{\boldsymbol{K}}_{1}^{-2}\|

\displaystyle\leq C\sqrt{(\log d)/d}\times n^{1/2}[\lambda_{\min}({\boldsymbol{K}})\wedge\lambda_{\min}({\boldsymbol{K}}_{1})]^{-3}\|{\boldsymbol{K}}-{\boldsymbol{K}}_{1}\|

(261)

\displaystyle\leq C\sqrt{\log d}\|{\boldsymbol{K}}-{\boldsymbol{K}}_{1}\|\leq C\,n^{-c_{0}}\,.

Using again Lemma
A.2
together with
Lemma
A.9
, we obtain that the following
holds with very high probability:

\displaystyle\Big|\langle{\boldsymbol{B}},{\boldsymbol{K}}_{1}^{-2}\rangle-\langle{\boldsymbol{B}}_{0},{\boldsymbol{K}}_{1}^{-2}\rangle\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\lambda_{\min}({\boldsymbol{K}}_{1})^{-2}\Big\|{\boldsymbol{B}}-{\boldsymbol{B}}_{0}\Big\|_{*}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{d^{1/2}}\,.

The desired claim follows from this display alongside Eq. (
261
).
∎

Lemma A.11
.

Under the assumptions of Theorem
4.13
, let
{\boldsymbol{a}}
be defined as in Lemma
A.3
.
Then, with very high probability we have

\displaystyle 0\leq\langle{\boldsymbol{a}},{\boldsymbol{K}}^{-2}{\boldsymbol{a}}\rangle\leq\frac{C}{n}\,.

(262)

Proof.

Notice that the lower bound is trivial since
{\boldsymbol{K}}
is positive semidefinite.
We will write

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{K}}

\displaystyle=\alpha\,{\boldsymbol{1}}{\boldsymbol{1}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{\,T}}}+{\boldsymbol{K}}_{*}\,,

(263)

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{a}}

\displaystyle=h(0){\boldsymbol{1}}+\tilde{\boldsymbol{a}}\,.

(264)

By standard bounds on the norm of matrices with i.i.d. rows (and using
\|{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}\|\leq C
), we have

0\preceq{\boldsymbol{X}}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{\,T}}/d\preceq C\,{\mathbf{I}}
, with probability at least
1-C\exp(-n/C)
.
Therefore, by Lemma
A.2
, and since
\beta\gamma>0
is bounded away from zero by assumption,
with very high probability we have
C^{-1}{\mathbf{I}}\preceq{\boldsymbol{K}}_{*}\preceq C{\mathbf{I}}
, for a suitable constant
C
.
Note that
\tilde{\boldsymbol{a}}=({\boldsymbol{a}}_{0}-h(0){\boldsymbol{1}})+{\boldsymbol{a}}_{1}
.
Under event
{\mathcal{E}}_{1}\cap{\mathcal{E}}_{2}
, the following holds by smoothness of
h
:

\displaystyle\|{\boldsymbol{a}}_{0}-h(0){\boldsymbol{1}}\|_{\infty}=\max_{i\leq d}\left|\mathbb{E}\left\{h\Big(\sqrt{\frac{Q_{ii}}{d}}G\Big)-h(0)\right\}\right|\leq\frac{C}{d}\,.

(265)

On the other hand, recalling the definition of
{\boldsymbol{a}}_{1}
in Eq. (
178
), we have, always on
{\mathcal{E}}_{1}\cap{\mathcal{E}}_{2}
,

\displaystyle\|{\boldsymbol{a}}_{1}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{1}{d^{3/2}}\max_{i\leq d}Q^{3/2}_{ii}\times d\times\max_{i\leq n}\frac{\|{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{1/2}{\boldsymbol{x}}_{i}\|_{\infty}^{3}}{\|{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{1/2}{\boldsymbol{x}}_{i}\|_{2}^{3}}

(266)

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{1}{d^{3/2}}\times d\times\Big(\frac{\log d}{d}\Big)^{3/2}\leq C\frac{(\log d)^{3/2}}{d^{2}}\,.

(267)

Therefore we conclude that
\|\tilde{\boldsymbol{a}}\|_{\infty}\leq C/d
, whence
\|\tilde{\boldsymbol{a}}\|_{2}\leq C/\sqrt{d}
.

We therefore obtain, again using Lemma
A.2
,

\displaystyle\Big|\langle{\boldsymbol{a}},{\boldsymbol{K}}^{-2}{\boldsymbol{a}}\rangle-h(0)^{2}\langle{\boldsymbol{1}},{\boldsymbol{K}}^{-2}{\boldsymbol{1}}\rangle-2h(0)\langle{\boldsymbol{1}},{\boldsymbol{K}}^{-2}\tilde{\boldsymbol{a}}\rangle\Big|=\langle\tilde{\boldsymbol{a}},{\boldsymbol{K}}^{-2}\tilde{\boldsymbol{a}}\rangle\leq\lambda_{\min}({\boldsymbol{K}})^{-2}\|\tilde{\boldsymbol{a}}\|_{2}^{2}\leq\frac{C}{d}\,.

(268)

We are therefore left with the task of controlling the two terms
\langle{\boldsymbol{1}},{\boldsymbol{K}}^{-2}{\boldsymbol{1}}\rangle
and
\langle\tilde{\boldsymbol{a}},{\boldsymbol{K}}^{-2}{\boldsymbol{1}}\rangle
.
We will assume
h(0)\neq 0
because otherwise there is nothing to control. Since
h
is a positive semidefinite kernel, this
also implies
h(0)>0
and
\alpha\geq h(0)>0
. By an application of the Sherman-Morrison formula, we get

\displaystyle\langle{\boldsymbol{1}},{\boldsymbol{K}}^{-2}{\boldsymbol{1}}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle{\boldsymbol{1}},({\boldsymbol{K}}_{*}+\alpha{\boldsymbol{1}}{\boldsymbol{1}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{\,T}}})^{-2}{\boldsymbol{1}}\rangle

(269)

\displaystyle=\frac{\langle{\boldsymbol{1}},{\boldsymbol{K}}_{*}^{-2}{\boldsymbol{1}}\rangle}{(1+\alpha\langle{\boldsymbol{1}},{\boldsymbol{K}}_{*}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{1}}\rangle)^{2}}

(270)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\alpha^{2}}\frac{\langle{\boldsymbol{1}},{\boldsymbol{K}}_{*}^{-2}{\boldsymbol{1}}\rangle}{\langle{\boldsymbol{1}},{\boldsymbol{K}}_{*}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{1}}\rangle^{2}}\leq\frac{C}{\alpha^{2}}\frac{1}{\|{\boldsymbol{1}}\|^{2}}\leq\frac{C}{d}\,,

(271)

where we used the above remark
C^{-1}{\mathbf{I}}\preceq{\boldsymbol{K}}_{*}\preceq C{\mathbf{I}}
.

Using again Sherman-Morrison formula,

\displaystyle\langle{\boldsymbol{1}},{\boldsymbol{K}}^{-2}\tilde{\boldsymbol{a}}\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{\langle\tilde{\boldsymbol{a}},{\boldsymbol{K}}_{*}^{-2}{\boldsymbol{1}}\rangle}{1+\alpha\langle{\boldsymbol{1}},{\boldsymbol{K}}_{*}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{1}}\rangle}-\frac{\alpha\langle{\boldsymbol{1}},{\boldsymbol{K}}_{*}^{-2}{\boldsymbol{1}}\rangle\langle\tilde{\boldsymbol{a}},{\boldsymbol{K}}_{*}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{1}}\rangle}{(1+\alpha\langle{\boldsymbol{1}},{\boldsymbol{K}}_{*}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{1}}\rangle)^{2}}\,,

(272)

\displaystyle\big|\langle{\boldsymbol{1}},{\boldsymbol{K}}^{-2}\tilde{\boldsymbol{a}}\rangle\big|

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{\|\tilde{\boldsymbol{a}}\|_{2}\|{\boldsymbol{1}}\|_{2}}{\alpha\|{\boldsymbol{1}}\|_{2}^{2}}+\frac{\|{\boldsymbol{1}}\|_{2}^{3}\|\tilde{\boldsymbol{a}}\|_{2}}{\alpha\|{\boldsymbol{1}}\|_{2}^{4}}

(273)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{d}\,.

(274)

Using the last two displays in Eq. (
268
) yields the desired claim.
∎

Proof of Theorem
4.13
: Variance term.

By virtue of Lemmas
A.8
,
A.10
,
A.11
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sigma^{2}_{\xi}}{\widehat{\textsc{var}}}

\displaystyle=\langle{\boldsymbol{B}}_{0},{\boldsymbol{K}}_{1}^{-2}\rangle+{\rm Err}(n)

(275)

\displaystyle=\langle{\boldsymbol{B}}_{0},({\boldsymbol{K}}_{0}+\alpha{\boldsymbol{1}}{\boldsymbol{1}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{\,T}}})^{-2}\rangle+{\rm Err}(n)\,.

(276)

Here and below we denote by
{\rm Err}(n)
an error term bounded as
|{\rm Err}(n)|\leq Cn^{-c_{0}}
with very high probability, and we defined

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{K}}_{0}

\displaystyle:=\beta\frac{{\boldsymbol{X}}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{\,T}}}{d}+\beta\gamma\mathbf{I}_{n}\,.

(277)

By an application of the Sherman-Morrison formula, and recalling that
\beta\gamma>0
is bounded away from zero, we get

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sigma_{\xi}^{2}}{\widehat{\textsc{var}}}=

\displaystyle\mathsf{tr}({\boldsymbol{B}}_{0}{\boldsymbol{K}}_{0}^{-2})-\frac{2\alpha}{1+\alpha A_{1}}\,\mathsf{tr}({\boldsymbol{B}}_{0}{\boldsymbol{K}}_{0}^{-2}{\boldsymbol{1}}{\boldsymbol{1}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{\,T}}}{\boldsymbol{K}}_{0}^{-1})

(278)

\displaystyle+\frac{\alpha^{2}A_{2}}{(1+\alpha A_{1})^{2}}\,\mathsf{tr}({\boldsymbol{B}}_{0}{\boldsymbol{K}}_{0}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{1}}{\boldsymbol{1}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{\,T}}}{\boldsymbol{K}}_{0}^{-1})+{\rm Err}(n)\,,

(279)

where
A_{\ell}:=\langle{\boldsymbol{1}},{\boldsymbol{K}}_{0}^{-\ell}{\boldsymbol{1}}\rangle
,
\ell\in\{1,2\}
. By standard bounds on the norm of matrices with i.i.d. rows
(and using
\|{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}\|\leq C
), we have

0\preceq{\boldsymbol{X}}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{\,T}}/d\preceq C\,{\mathbf{I}}
. Therefore
C^{-1}{\mathbf{I}}\preceq{\boldsymbol{K}}_{0}\preceq C{\mathbf{I}}
, for a suitable constant
C
,
with very high probability. This implies
d/C\leq A_{\ell}\leq Cd
for
\ell\in\{1,2\}
and some constant
C>0
.
Further
\|{\boldsymbol{B}}_{0}\|\leq C\|{\boldsymbol{X}}\|^{2}/d^{2}\leq C/d
.
Therefore, (since
\alpha>0
):

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{\sigma_{\xi}^{2}}{\widehat{\textsc{var}}}-\mathsf{tr}({\boldsymbol{B}}_{0}{\boldsymbol{K}}_{0}^{-2})\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{d}\,\big|\langle{\boldsymbol{1}},{\boldsymbol{K}}_{0}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{B}}_{0}{\boldsymbol{K}}_{0}^{-2}{\boldsymbol{1}}\rangle\big|+\frac{C}{d}\,\langle{\boldsymbol{1}},{\boldsymbol{K}}_{0}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{B}}_{0}{\boldsymbol{K}}_{0}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{1}}\rangle+{\rm Err}(n)

(280)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{d}+{\rm Err}(n)\,.

(281)

We are therefore left with the task of evaluating the asymptotics of

\displaystyle\mathsf{tr}({\boldsymbol{B}}_{0}{\boldsymbol{K}}_{0}^{-2})=\mathsf{tr}\big({\boldsymbol{X}}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{\,T}}}({\boldsymbol{X}}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{\,T}}+\gamma d\mathbf{I}_{n})^{2}\big)\,.

(282)

However, this is just the variance of ridge regression with respect to the simple features
{\boldsymbol{X}}
, with ridge regularization
proportional to
\gamma
. We apply the results of
[
HMRT20
]
to obtain the claim.
∎

A.2.4
Proof of Theorem
4.13
: Bias term

We recall the decomposition

\displaystyle f^{*}({\boldsymbol{x}})=b_{0}+\langle{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0},{\boldsymbol{x}}\rangle+f^{*}_{\mbox{\rm\tiny NL}}({\boldsymbol{x}})=:f^{*}_{\mbox{\rm\tiny L}}({\boldsymbol{x}})+f^{*}_{\mbox{\rm\tiny NL}}({\boldsymbol{x}})\,,

(283)

where
b_{0}
,
{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0}
are defined by the orthogonality conditions
\mathbb{E}\{f^{*}_{\mbox{\rm\tiny NL}}({\boldsymbol{x}})\}=\mathbb{E}\{{\boldsymbol{x}}f^{*}_{\mbox{\rm\tiny NL}}({\boldsymbol{x}})\}=0
.
This yields
b_{0}=\mathbb{E}\{f^{*}({\boldsymbol{x}})\}
and
{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0}={\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{-1}\mathbb{E}\{f^{*}({\boldsymbol{x}}){\boldsymbol{x}}\}
.
We denote by
{\boldsymbol{f}}^{*}=(f^{*}({\boldsymbol{x}}_{1}),\dots,f^{*}({\boldsymbol{x}}_{n}))^{{\mathsf{T}}}
the vector of noiseless responses, which we correspondingly decompose as

{\boldsymbol{f}}^{*}={\boldsymbol{f}}^{*}_{\mbox{\rm\tiny L}}+{\boldsymbol{f}}^{*}_{\mbox{\rm\tiny NL}}
. Recalling the definition of
{\boldsymbol{M}}
,
{\boldsymbol{v}}
in Eqs. (
173
), (
174
), the bias reads

\displaystyle{\widehat{\textsc{bias}}^{2}}

\displaystyle=\langle{\boldsymbol{f}}^{*},{\boldsymbol{K}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{M}}{\boldsymbol{K}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{f}}^{*}\rangle-2\langle{\boldsymbol{v}},{\boldsymbol{K}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{f}}^{*}\rangle+\|f^{*}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\,.

(284)

We begin with an elementary lemma on the norm of
{\boldsymbol{f}}^{*}
.

Lemma A.12
.

Assume
\mathbb{E}\{f^{*}({\boldsymbol{x}})^{4}\}\leq C_{0}
for a constant
C_{0}
(in particular, this is the case if
\mathbb{E}\{|f^{*}({\boldsymbol{x}})|^{4+\eta}\}\leq C_{0}
). Then,
there exists a constant
C
depending uniquely on
C_{0}
such that the following hold:

(a)

|b_{0}|\leq C
,
\|{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{1/2}{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0}\|_{2}\leq C
,
\mathbb{E}\{f^{*}_{\mbox{\rm\tiny NL}}({\boldsymbol{x}})^{2}\}\leq C
.

(b)

With probability at least
1-Cn^{-1/4}
, we have
|\|{\boldsymbol{f}}^{*}\|_{2}^{2}/n-\|f^{*}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}|\leq n^{-3/8}
.

(c)

With probability at least
1-Cn^{-1/4}
, we have
|\|{\boldsymbol{f}}_{\mbox{\rm\tiny NL}}^{*}\|_{2}^{2}/n-\|f_{\mbox{\rm\tiny NL}}^{*}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}|\leq n^{-3/8}
.

Proof.

By Jensen’s inequality we have
\mathbb{E}\{f^{*}({\boldsymbol{x}})^{2}\}\leq C
. By orthogonality of
f^{*}_{\mbox{\rm\tiny NL}}
to linear and constant functions, we
also have
\mathbb{E}\{f^{*}({\boldsymbol{x}})^{2}\}=b_{0}^{2}+\mathbb{E}\{\langle{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0},{\boldsymbol{x}}\rangle^{2}\}+\mathbb{E}\{f^{*}_{\mbox{\rm\tiny NL}}({\boldsymbol{x}})^{2}\}=b_{0}^{2}+\|{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{1/2}{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0}\|_{2}^{2}+\mathbb{E}\{f^{*}_{\mbox{\rm\tiny NL}}({\boldsymbol{x}})^{2}\}
,
which proves claim
(a)
.

To prove
(b)
, simply call
Z=\|{\boldsymbol{f}}^{*}\|_{2}^{2}/n-\|f^{*}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}
, and note that
\mathbb{E}\{Z^{2}\}=(\mathbb{E}\{f^{*}({\boldsymbol{x}})^{4}\}-\mathbb{E}\{f^{*}({\boldsymbol{x}})^{2}\}^{2})/n\leq C/n
.
The claim follows by Chebyshev inequality.

Finally,
(c)
follows by the same argument as for claim
(b)
, once we bound
\|f^{*}_{\mbox{\rm\tiny NL}}\|_{L^{4}}
. In order to show this, notice
that, by triangle inequality,
\|f^{*}_{\mbox{\rm\tiny NL}}\|_{L^{4}}\leq\|f^{*}\|_{L^{4}}+\|f_{0}\|_{L^{4}}+\|f_{1}\|_{L^{4}}
, where
f_{0}({\boldsymbol{x}})=b_{0}
,
f_{1}({\boldsymbol{x}})=\langle{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0},{\boldsymbol{x}}\rangle
.
Since
{\boldsymbol{x}}={\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}{\boldsymbol{z}}
, with
{\boldsymbol{z}}

C
-sub-Gaussian,
\|f^{*}_{\mbox{\rm\tiny NL}}\|_{L^{4}}\leq\|f^{*}\|_{L^{4}}+b_{0}+C\|{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{1/2}{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0}\|_{2}\leq C
.
∎

Lemma A.13
.

Under the assumptions of Theorem
4.13
, let
{\boldsymbol{M}}_{0}
,
{\boldsymbol{v}}_{0}
be defined as in the statement of Lemma

A.3
. Then, with probability at least
1-Cn^{-1/4}
, we have

\displaystyle\big|{\widehat{\textsc{bias}}^{2}}-{\widehat{\textsc{bias}}^{2}}_{0}\big|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C\log d}{\sqrt{d}}\,,

(285)

\displaystyle{\widehat{\textsc{bias}}^{2}}_{0}

\displaystyle:=\langle{\boldsymbol{f}}^{*},{\boldsymbol{K}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{M}}_{0}{\boldsymbol{K}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{f}}^{*}\rangle-2\langle{\boldsymbol{v}}_{0},{\boldsymbol{K}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{f}}^{*}\rangle+\|f^{*}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\

(286)

Proof.

We have

\displaystyle\big|{\widehat{\textsc{bias}}^{2}}-{\widehat{\textsc{bias}}^{2}}_{0}\big|

\displaystyle\leq\big|\langle{\boldsymbol{f}}^{*},{\boldsymbol{K}}^{-1}({\boldsymbol{M}}-{\boldsymbol{M}}_{0}){\boldsymbol{K}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{f}}^{*}\rangle\big|+2\big|\langle{\boldsymbol{v}}-{\boldsymbol{v}}_{0},{\boldsymbol{K}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{f}}^{*}\rangle\big|

(287)

\displaystyle\leq\|{\boldsymbol{M}}-{\boldsymbol{M}}_{0}\|_{F}\|{\boldsymbol{K}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{f}}^{*}\|^{2}_{2}+2\|{\boldsymbol{v}}-{\boldsymbol{v}}_{0}\|_{2}\|{\boldsymbol{K}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{f}}^{*}\|_{2}

(288)

\displaystyle\leq\|{\boldsymbol{M}}-{\boldsymbol{M}}_{0}\|_{F}\|{\boldsymbol{K}}^{-1}\|^{2}\|{\boldsymbol{f}}^{*}\|^{2}_{2}+2\|{\boldsymbol{v}}-{\boldsymbol{v}}_{0}\|_{2}\|{\boldsymbol{K}}^{-1}\|\|{\boldsymbol{f}}^{*}\|_{2}

(289)

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{\log d}{d^{3/2}}\times n+C\frac{\log d}{d}\times\sqrt{n}\leq\frac{C\log d}{\sqrt{d}}\,.

(290)

Here, in the last line, we used Lemmas
A.2
,
A.3
and the fact that
\|{\boldsymbol{f}}^{*}\|^{2}\leq Cn
by Lemma
A.12
.
∎

In view of the last lemma, it is sufficient to work with
{\widehat{\textsc{bias}}^{2}}_{0}
. We decompose it as

\displaystyle{\widehat{\textsc{bias}}^{2}}_{0}

\displaystyle={\widehat{\textsc{bias}}^{2}}_{\mbox{\rm\tiny L}}+{\widehat{\textsc{bias}}^{2}}_{\mbox{\rm\tiny NL}}+{\widehat{\textsc{bias}}^{2}}_{\mbox{\rm\tiny mix}}+\|f^{*}_{\mbox{\rm\tiny NL}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\,,

(291)

\displaystyle{\widehat{\textsc{bias}}^{2}}_{\mbox{\rm\tiny L}}

\displaystyle:=\langle{\boldsymbol{f}}_{\mbox{\rm\tiny L}}^{*},{\boldsymbol{K}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{M}}_{0}{\boldsymbol{K}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{f}}_{\mbox{\rm\tiny L}}^{*}\rangle-2\langle{\boldsymbol{v}}_{0},{\boldsymbol{K}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{f}}_{\mbox{\rm\tiny L}}^{*}\rangle+\|f^{*}_{\mbox{\rm\tiny L}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\,,

(292)

\displaystyle{\widehat{\textsc{bias}}^{2}}_{\mbox{\rm\tiny NL}}

\displaystyle:=\langle{\boldsymbol{f}}_{\mbox{\rm\tiny NL}}^{*},{\boldsymbol{K}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{M}}_{0}{\boldsymbol{K}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{f}}_{\mbox{\rm\tiny NL}}^{*}\rangle\,,

(293)

\displaystyle{\widehat{\textsc{bias}}^{2}}_{\mbox{\rm\tiny mix}}

\displaystyle:=2\langle{\boldsymbol{f}}_{\mbox{\rm\tiny L}}^{*},{\boldsymbol{K}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{M}}_{0}{\boldsymbol{K}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{f}}_{\mbox{\rm\tiny NL}}^{*}\rangle-2\langle{\boldsymbol{v}}_{0},{\boldsymbol{K}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{f}}_{\mbox{\rm\tiny NL}}^{*}\rangle\,.

(294)

We next show that the contribution of the constant term in
f^{*}_{\mbox{\rm\tiny L}}({\boldsymbol{x}})
and
{\boldsymbol{M}}_{0}
is negligible.

Lemma A.14
.

Under the assumptions of Theorem
4.13
, let

{\boldsymbol{M}}_{0}
,
{\boldsymbol{B}}
,
{\boldsymbol{v}}_{0}
be defined as in the statement of
Lemma
A.3
. Further define

\displaystyle R_{\mbox{\rm\tiny L}}

\displaystyle:=\langle{\boldsymbol{X}}{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0},{\boldsymbol{K}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{B}}{\boldsymbol{K}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{X}}{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0}\rangle-\frac{2h^{\prime}(0)}{d}\langle{\boldsymbol{X}}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0},{\boldsymbol{K}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{X}}{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0}\rangle+\langle{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0},{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0}\rangle\,,

(295)

\displaystyle R_{\mbox{\rm\tiny NL}}

\displaystyle:=\langle{\boldsymbol{f}}_{\mbox{\rm\tiny NL}}^{*},{\boldsymbol{K}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{B}}{\boldsymbol{K}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{f}}_{\mbox{\rm\tiny NL}}^{*}\rangle\,,

(296)

\displaystyle R_{\mbox{\rm\tiny mix}}

\displaystyle:=2\langle{\boldsymbol{X}}{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0},{\boldsymbol{K}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{B}}{\boldsymbol{K}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{f}}_{\mbox{\rm\tiny NL}}^{*}\rangle-\frac{2h^{\prime}(0)}{d}\langle{\boldsymbol{X}}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0},{\boldsymbol{K}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{f}}_{\mbox{\rm\tiny NL}}^{*}\rangle\,.

(297)

Then, with very high probability we have

\displaystyle\big|{\widehat{\textsc{bias}}^{2}}_{\mbox{\rm\tiny L}}-R_{\mbox{\rm\tiny L}}\big|\leq\frac{C}{n}\,,

(298)

\displaystyle\big|{\widehat{\textsc{bias}}^{2}}_{\mbox{\rm\tiny NL}}-R_{\mbox{\rm\tiny NL}}\big|\leq\frac{C}{n}\,,

(299)

\displaystyle\big|{\widehat{\textsc{bias}}^{2}}_{\mbox{\rm\tiny mix}}-R_{\mbox{\rm\tiny mix}}\big|\leq\frac{C}{n}\,.

(300)

Proof.

The proof of this lemma is very similar to the one of Lemma
A.11
, and we omit it.
∎

Lemma A.15
.

Under the assumptions of Theorem
4.13
, let
\mathscrsfs{B}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}},{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0})
be defined as in
Eq. (
168
), and
R_{\mbox{\rm\tiny L}}
be defined as in the statement of Lemma

A.14
. Let
a\in(0,1/2)
. Then we have, with very high probability

\displaystyle\big|R_{\mbox{\rm\tiny L}}-\mathscrsfs{B}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}},{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0})\big|\leq C\,n^{-a}\,.

(301)

Proof.

Recall the definition of
{\boldsymbol{K}}_{1}
in Eq. (
163
). and define
\tilde{R}_{\mbox{\rm\tiny L}}
as
R_{\mbox{\rm\tiny L}}

(cf. Eq. (
295
)) except with
{\boldsymbol{B}}
replaced by
{\boldsymbol{B}}_{0}
defined in Eq. (
256
), and

{\boldsymbol{K}}
replaced by
{\boldsymbol{K}}_{1}
defined in Eq. (
163
). Namely:

\displaystyle\tilde{R}_{\mbox{\rm\tiny L}}

\displaystyle:=\langle{\boldsymbol{X}}{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0},{\boldsymbol{K}}_{1}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{B}}_{0}{\boldsymbol{K}}_{1}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{X}}{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0}\rangle-\frac{2h^{\prime}(0)}{d}\langle{\boldsymbol{X}}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0},{\boldsymbol{K}}_{1}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{X}}{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0}\rangle+\langle{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0},{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0}\rangle\,.

(302)

Letting
{\boldsymbol{u}}={\boldsymbol{X}}{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0}={\boldsymbol{Z}}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{1/2}{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0}
, note that
\|{\boldsymbol{u}}\|_{2}\leq\|{\boldsymbol{Z}}\|\|{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{1/2}{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0}\|_{2}\leq C\sqrt{n}
with very high probability (using Lemma
A.12
). We then have

\displaystyle\big|R_{\mbox{\rm\tiny L}}-\tilde{R}_{\mbox{\rm\tiny L}}\big|

\displaystyle\leq\big|\langle{\boldsymbol{u}},{\boldsymbol{K}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{B}}{\boldsymbol{K}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{u}}\rangle-\langle{\boldsymbol{u}},{\boldsymbol{K}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{B}}_{0}{\boldsymbol{K}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{u}}\rangle\big|

\displaystyle+\big|\langle{\boldsymbol{u}},{\boldsymbol{K}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{B}}_{0}{\boldsymbol{K}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{u}}\rangle-\langle{\boldsymbol{u}},{\boldsymbol{K}}_{1}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{B}}_{0}{\boldsymbol{K}}_{1}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{u}}\rangle\big|+\frac{C}{d}\big|\langle{\boldsymbol{X}}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0},{\boldsymbol{K}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{u}}\rangle-\langle{\boldsymbol{X}}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0},{\boldsymbol{K}}_{1}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{u}}\rangle\big|

\displaystyle=:E_{1}+E_{2}+E_{3}\,.

We bound each of the three terms with very high
probability:

\displaystyle E_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\|{\boldsymbol{B}}-{\boldsymbol{B}}_{0}\|\cdot\|{\boldsymbol{K}}^{-1}\|^{2}\cdot\|{\boldsymbol{u}}\|_{2}^{2}\leq\frac{C}{d^{3/2}}\times C\times Cn\leq\frac{C}{n^{1/2}}\,,

(303)

\displaystyle E_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\big(\|{\boldsymbol{B}}_{0}{\boldsymbol{K}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{u}}\|_{2}+\|{\boldsymbol{B}}_{0}{\boldsymbol{K}}_{1}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{u}}\|_{2}\big)\|{\boldsymbol{u}}\|_{2}\|{\boldsymbol{K}}^{-1}-{\boldsymbol{K}}^{-1}_{1}\|

\displaystyle\leq\|{\boldsymbol{B}}_{0}\|\big(\|{\boldsymbol{K}}^{-1}\|+\|{\boldsymbol{K}}_{1}^{-1}\|\big)\|{\boldsymbol{u}}\|^{2}_{2}\|{\boldsymbol{K}}^{-1}-{\boldsymbol{K}}^{-1}_{1}\|

(304)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{d}\times C\times Cn\times n^{-c_{0}}\leq C\,n^{-c_{0}}\,,

\displaystyle E_{3}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{d}\|{\boldsymbol{X}}\|\|{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0}\|_{2}\|{\boldsymbol{u}}\|_{2}\|{\boldsymbol{K}}^{-1}-{\boldsymbol{K}}^{-1}_{1}\|

(305)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{d}\times C\sqrt{n}\times C\times C\sqrt{n}\times Cn^{-c_{0}}\leq Cn^{-c_{0}}\,.

Here in Eq. (
303
) we used Lemma
A.2
and Lemma
A.9
; in Eq. (
304
) Lemma
A.2

and the fact that
\|{\boldsymbol{B}}_{0}\|\leq C/d
; in Eq. (
305
), Lemma
A.2
and
\|{\boldsymbol{X}}\|\leq C\sqrt{d}
.
Hence we conclude that

\displaystyle\big|R_{\mbox{\rm\tiny L}}-\tilde{R}_{\mbox{\rm\tiny L}}\big|

\displaystyle\leq Cn^{-c_{0}}\,.

(306)

Finally define
\accentset{\approx}{R}_{\mbox{\rm\tiny L}}
as
\tilde{R}_{\mbox{\rm\tiny L}}
, with
{\boldsymbol{K}}_{1}
replaced by
{\boldsymbol{K}}_{0}=\beta\frac{{\boldsymbol{X}}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{\,T}}}{d}+\beta\gamma\mathbf{I}_{n}
.

\displaystyle\big|\tilde{R}_{\mbox{\rm\tiny L}}-\accentset{\approx}{R}_{\mbox{\rm\tiny L}}\big|

\displaystyle\leq\big|\langle{\boldsymbol{u}},({\boldsymbol{K}}_{1}^{-1}+{\boldsymbol{K}}_{0}^{-1}){\boldsymbol{B}}_{0}({\boldsymbol{K}}_{1}^{-1}-{\boldsymbol{K}}_{0}^{-1}){\boldsymbol{u}}\rangle\big|+\frac{C}{d}\big|\langle{\boldsymbol{X}}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0},({\boldsymbol{K}}^{-1}_{1}-{\boldsymbol{K}}_{0}^{-1}){\boldsymbol{u}}\rangle\big|

\displaystyle=:G_{1}+G_{2}\,.

By the Sherman-Morrison formula, for any two vectors
{\boldsymbol{w}}_{1},{\boldsymbol{w}}_{2}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
, we have

\displaystyle\big|\langle{\boldsymbol{w}}_{1},({\boldsymbol{K}}_{1}^{-1}-{\boldsymbol{K}}_{0}^{-1}){\boldsymbol{w}}_{2}\rangle\big|

\displaystyle=\alpha\frac{\big|\langle{\boldsymbol{1}},{\boldsymbol{K}}_{0}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{w}}_{1}\rangle\langle{\boldsymbol{1}},{\boldsymbol{K}}_{0}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{w}}_{2}\rangle\big|}{1+\alpha\langle{\boldsymbol{1}},{\boldsymbol{K}}_{0}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{1}}\rangle}

(307)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{d}\big|\langle{\boldsymbol{1}},{\boldsymbol{K}}_{0}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{w}}_{1}\rangle\big|\cdot\big|\langle{\boldsymbol{1}},{\boldsymbol{K}}_{0}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{w}}_{2}\rangle\big|\,.

(308)

Further notice that

\displaystyle|\langle{\boldsymbol{u}},{\boldsymbol{K}}^{-1}_{0},{\boldsymbol{1}}\rangle|=|\langle{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0},{\boldsymbol{X}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{\,T}}}(\beta{\boldsymbol{X}}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{\,T}}}/d+\beta\gamma\mathbf{I}_{n})^{-1}{\boldsymbol{1}}\rangle|\leq C\sqrt{d\log d}\,,

where the last inequality holds with very high probability by
[
KY17
, Theorem 3.16]
(cf. also Lemma 4.4 in the same paper).
We therefore have

\displaystyle G_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{d}\big|\langle{\boldsymbol{u}},({\boldsymbol{K}}_{1}^{-1}+{\boldsymbol{K}}_{0}^{-1}){\boldsymbol{B}}_{0}{\boldsymbol{K}}_{0}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{1}}\rangle\big|\cdot\big|\langle{\boldsymbol{u}},{\boldsymbol{K}}_{0}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{1}}\rangle\big|

(309)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{d}\|{\boldsymbol{B}}_{0}\|\|{\boldsymbol{u}}\|_{2}\|\mathbf{1}\|_{2}\big|\langle{\boldsymbol{u}},{\boldsymbol{K}}_{0}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{1}}\rangle\big|

(310)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{d}\times\frac{1}{d}\times\sqrt{d}\times\sqrt{d}\times\sqrt{d\log d}\leq C\sqrt{\frac{\log d}{d}}\,.

(311)

Analogously

\displaystyle G_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{d^{2}}\big|\langle{\boldsymbol{X}}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0},{\boldsymbol{K}}_{0}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{1}}\rangle\big|\cdot\big|\langle{\boldsymbol{u}},{\boldsymbol{K}}_{0}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{1}}\rangle\big|

(312)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{d^{2}}\|{\boldsymbol{X}}\|\|{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0}\|_{2}\|{\boldsymbol{K}}_{0}^{-1}\|\|{\boldsymbol{1}}\|_{2}\big|\langle{\boldsymbol{u}},{\boldsymbol{K}}_{0}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{1}}\rangle\big|

(313)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{d^{2}}\times C\sqrt{d}\times C\times C\times C\sqrt{n}\times C\sqrt{d\log d}\leq C\sqrt{\frac{\log d}{d}}\,.

(314)

Summarizing

\displaystyle\big|\tilde{R}_{\mbox{\rm\tiny L}}-\accentset{\approx}{R}_{\mbox{\rm\tiny L}}\big|

\displaystyle\leq C\sqrt{\frac{\log d}{d}}\,.

(315)

We are left with the task of estimating
\accentset{\approx}{R}_{\mbox{\rm\tiny L}}
which we rewrite explicitly as

\displaystyle\accentset{\approx}{R}_{\mbox{\rm\tiny L}}=\gamma^{2}\big\|{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{1/2}({\boldsymbol{X}}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{{\mathsf{T}}}+\gamma{\mathbf{I}}_{n})^{-1}{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0}\big\|_{2}^{2}\,.

(316)

We recognize in this the bias of ridge regression with respect to the linear features
{\boldsymbol{x}}_{i}
, when the responses are also linear

\langle{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0},{\boldsymbol{x}}_{i}\rangle
. Using the results of
[
HMRT20
]
, we obtain that, for any
a\in(0,1/2)
, the following holds
with very high probability.

\displaystyle\big|\accentset{\approx}{R}_{\mbox{\rm\tiny L}}-\mathscrsfs{B}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}},{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0})\big|\leq C\,n^{-c_{0}}\,.

(317)

The proof is completed by using Eqs. (
306
), (
315
), (
317
).
∎

We next consider the nonlinear term
R_{\mbox{\rm\tiny NL}}
, cf. Eq. (
296
).

Lemma A.16
.

Under the assumptions of Theorem
4.13
, let
\mathscrsfs{V}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}})
be defined as in
Eq. (
167
), and
R_{\mbox{\rm\tiny NL}}
be defined as in the statement of Lemma

A.14
. Then there exists
c_{0}>0
such that, with probability at least
1-Cn^{-1/4}
,

\displaystyle\big|R_{\mbox{\rm\tiny NL}}-\mathscrsfs{V}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}})\|{\mathsf{P}}_{>1}f^{*}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\big|\leq C\,n^{-c_{0}}\,.

(318)

Proof.

Define

\displaystyle\accentset{\approx}{R}_{\mbox{\rm\tiny NL}}

\displaystyle:=\langle{\boldsymbol{f}}_{\mbox{\rm\tiny NL}}^{*},{\boldsymbol{K}}_{0}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{B}}_{0}{\boldsymbol{K}}_{0}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{f}}_{\mbox{\rm\tiny NL}}^{*}\rangle

(319)
