\displaystyle=\frac{1}{d^{2}}\langle{\boldsymbol{f}}_{\mbox{\rm\tiny NL}}^{*},({\boldsymbol{X}}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{\,T}}}/d+\gamma{\mathbf{I}}_{n})^{-1}{\boldsymbol{X}}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{\,T}}}({\boldsymbol{X}}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{\,T}}}/d+\gamma{\mathbf{I}}_{n})^{-1}{\boldsymbol{f}}_{\mbox{\rm\tiny NL}}^{*}\rangle

(320)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{d^{2}}\langle{\boldsymbol{f}}_{\mbox{\rm\tiny NL}}^{*},({\boldsymbol{Z}}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}{\boldsymbol{Z}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{\,T}}}/d+\gamma{\mathbf{I}}_{n})^{-1}{\boldsymbol{Z}}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{2}{\boldsymbol{Z}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{\,T}}}({\boldsymbol{Z}}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}{\boldsymbol{Z}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{\,T}}}/d+\gamma{\mathbf{I}}_{n})^{-1}{\boldsymbol{f}}_{\mbox{\rm\tiny NL}}^{*}\rangle\,.

(321)

By the same argument as in the proof of Lemma
A.15
, we have, with very high probability,

\displaystyle\big|R_{\mbox{\rm\tiny NL}}-\accentset{\approx}{R}_{\mbox{\rm\tiny NL}}\big|

\displaystyle\leq C\sqrt{\frac{\log d}{d}}\,.

(322)

We next use the following identity, which holds for any two symmetric matrices
{\boldsymbol{A}}
,
{\boldsymbol{M}}
, and any
t\neq 0
,

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{M}}{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}=\frac{1}{t}\big[{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}-({\boldsymbol{A}}+t{\boldsymbol{M}})^{-1}\big]+t{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{M}}{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{M}}({\boldsymbol{A}}+t{\boldsymbol{M}})^{-1}\,.

(323)

Therefore, for any matrix
{\boldsymbol{U}}
and any
t>0
, we have

\displaystyle\big|\langle{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{M}}{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1},{\boldsymbol{U}}\rangle\big|\leq\frac{1}{t}\big|\langle{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1},{\boldsymbol{U}}\rangle\big|+\frac{1}{t}\big|\langle({\boldsymbol{A}}+t{\boldsymbol{M}})^{-1},{\boldsymbol{U}}\rangle\big|+t\|{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}\|^{2}\|{\boldsymbol{M}}\|^{2}\|({\boldsymbol{A}}+t{\boldsymbol{M}})^{-1}\|\|{\boldsymbol{U}}\|_{*}\,.

(324)

We apply this inequality to
{\boldsymbol{A}}={\boldsymbol{Z}}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}{\boldsymbol{Z}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{\,T}}}/d+\gamma{\mathbf{I}}_{n}
,

{\boldsymbol{M}}={\boldsymbol{Z}}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{2}{\boldsymbol{Z}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{\,T}}}/d
and
U_{ij}=f^{*}_{\mbox{\rm\tiny NL}}({\boldsymbol{x}}_{i})f^{*}_{\mbox{\rm\tiny NL}}({\boldsymbol{x}}_{i}){\boldsymbol{1}}_{i\neq j}
.
Note that
\|{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}\|,\|{\boldsymbol{M}}\|,\|({\boldsymbol{A}}+t{\boldsymbol{M}})^{-1}\|\leq C
. Further

\|{\boldsymbol{U}}\|_{*}\leq 2\|{\boldsymbol{f}}^{*}_{\mbox{\rm\tiny NL}}\|_{2}^{2}\leq Cn
with probability at least
1-Cn^{-1/4}
by Lemma
A.12
.
Finally for any
t\in(0,1)
, by Theorem
A.7
, the following hold
with probability at least
1-Cd^{-1/4}
:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{d}\big|\langle{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1},{\boldsymbol{U}}\rangle\big|\leq C\,d^{-1/8}\,,\;\;\;\;\;\;\frac{1}{d}\big|\langle({\boldsymbol{A}}+t{\boldsymbol{M}})^{-1},{\boldsymbol{U}}\rangle\big|\leq C\,d^{-1/8}\,.

(325)

Therefore, applying Eq. (
324
) we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{1}{d}\big|\langle{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{M}}{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1},{\boldsymbol{U}}\rangle\big|\leq\frac{1}{t}C\,d^{-1/8}+Ct\leq Cd^{-1/16}\,,

(326)

where in the last step we selected
t=d^{-1/16}
. Recalling the definitions of
{\boldsymbol{A}},{\boldsymbol{M}},{\boldsymbol{U}}
, we have proved:

\displaystyle\left|\accentset{\approx}{R}_{\mbox{\rm\tiny NL}}-\frac{1}{d^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}[{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{M}}{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}]_{ii}f_{\mbox{\rm\tiny NL}}^{*}({\boldsymbol{x}}_{i})^{2}\right|\leq Cd^{-1/16}\,.

(327)

We are therefore left with the task of controlling the diagonal terms. Using the results of
[
KY17
]
, we get

\displaystyle\max_{i\leq n}\left|[{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{M}}{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}]_{ii}-\frac{1}{n}\mathsf{tr}({\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{M}}{\boldsymbol{A}}^{-1})\right|\leq Cn^{-1/8}\,.

(328)

Further
|\|{\boldsymbol{f}}^{*}_{\mbox{\rm\tiny NL}}\|_{2}^{2}/n-\|f^{*}_{\mbox{\rm\tiny NL}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}|\leq Cn^{-1/2}
with probability at least
1-Cn^{-1/4}
by Lemma

A.12
. Therefore, with probability at least

1-Cd^{-1/4}
,

\displaystyle\left|\accentset{\approx}{R}_{\mbox{\rm\tiny NL}}-V_{\mbox{\tiny\rm RR}}\|f_{\mbox{\rm\tiny NL}}^{*}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right|\leq Cd^{-1/16}\,,

(329)

\displaystyle V_{\mbox{\tiny\rm RR}}:=\frac{1}{d^{2}}\big\|{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{1/2}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{\,T}}}({\boldsymbol{X}}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{\,T}}}/d+\gamma{\mathbf{I}}_{n})^{-1}\big\|_{F}^{2}\,.

(330)

We finally recognize that the term
V_{\mbox{\tiny\rm RR}}
is just the variance of ridge regression with respect to the linear features
{\boldsymbol{x}}_{i}
, and using
[
HMRT20
]
, we obtain

\displaystyle\left|\accentset{\approx}{R}_{\mbox{\rm\tiny NL}}-\mathscrsfs{V}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}})\|f_{\mbox{\rm\tiny NL}}^{*}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right|\leq Cd^{-1/16}\,.

(331)

The proof of the lemma is concluded by using the last equation together with Eq. (
322
).
∎

Lemma A.17
.

Under the assumptions of Theorem
4.13
,

R_{\mbox{\rm\tiny mix}}
be defined as in the statement of
Lemma
A.14
. Then we have, with
probability at least
1-Cd^{-1/4}
,

\displaystyle\big|R_{\mbox{\rm\tiny mix}}\big|\leq C\,n^{-1/16}\,.

(332)

Proof.

The proof of this lemma is analogous to the one of Lemma
A.16
and we omit it.
∎

We are now in a position to prove Theorem
4.13
.

Proof of Theorem
4.13
: Bias term.

Using Lemma
A.13
, Eq. (
291
) and Lemma
A.14
, we obtain
that, with very high probability,

\displaystyle\big|{\widehat{\textsc{bias}}^{2}}-(R_{\mbox{\rm\tiny L}}+R_{\mbox{\rm\tiny NL}}+R_{\mbox{\rm\tiny mix}}+\|f^{*}_{\mbox{\rm\tiny NL}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2})\big|\leq C\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n}}\,.

(333)

Hence the proof is completed by using Lemmas
A.15
,
A.16
,
A.17
.
∎

A.2.5
Consequences: Proof of Corollary 4.14

We denote by

\lambda_{1}\geq\dots\geq\lambda_{d}
the eigenvalues of
{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}
in decreasing order.

First note that the left hand side of Eq. (
166
) is strictly increasing in
\lambda_{*}
, while the right
hand side is strictly decreasing. By considering the limits as
\lambda_{*}\to 0
and
\lambda_{*}\to\infty
, it is easy to
see that this equation admits indeed a unique solution.

Next denoting by
F(x):={\mathsf{tr}}\Big({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}+x{\mathbf{I}})^{-1}\Big)

the function appearing on the right hand side of Eq. (
166
), we have, for
x\geq c_{*}\lambda_{k+1}
,

\displaystyle F(x)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{d}\frac{\lambda_{i}}{x+\lambda_{i}}\geq\sum_{i=k+1}^{d}\frac{\lambda_{i}}{x+\lambda_{i}}

(334)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{c_{*}}{(1+c_{*})x}\sum_{i=k+1}^{d}\lambda_{i}=:\underline{F}(x)\,.

(335)

Let
\underline{\lambda}_{*}
be the unique non-negative solution of
n(1-(\gamma/\underline{\lambda}_{*}))=\underline{F}(\underline{\lambda}_{*})
. Then, the above inequality
implies that whenever
\underline{\lambda}_{*}\geq c_{*}\lambda_{k+1}
we have
\lambda_{*}\geq\underline{\lambda}_{*}
.
Solving explicitly for
\underline{\lambda}_{*}
, we get

\displaystyle\frac{(1+c_{*})\gamma}{c_{*}\lambda_{k+1}}+\frac{r_{k}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}})}{n}\geq(1+c_{*})\;\;\Rightarrow\;\;\lambda_{*}\geq\gamma+\frac{c_{*}}{1+c_{*}}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=k+1}^{d}\lambda_{i}\,.

(336)

Next, we upper bound

\displaystyle{\mathsf{tr}}\big({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{2}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{*}{\mathbf{I}})^{-2}\big)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{d}\frac{\lambda_{i}^{2}}{(\lambda_{i}+\lambda_{*})^{2}}

(337)

\displaystyle\leq k+\frac{1}{\lambda_{*}^{2}}\sum_{i=k+1}^{d}\lambda_{i}^{2}

(338)

\displaystyle\leq k+(1+c_{*}^{-1})^{2}n^{2}\frac{\sum_{i=k+1}^{d}\lambda_{i}^{2}}{(n\gamma/c_{*}+\sum_{i=k+1}^{d}\lambda_{i})^{2}}\,.

(339)

If we assume that the right-hand side is less than
1/2
, using Theorem
4.13
, we
obtain that, with high probability,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sigma_{\xi}^{2}}{\widehat{\textsc{var}}}\leq k+(1+c_{*}^{-1})^{2}n^{2}\frac{\sum_{i=k+1}^{d}\lambda_{i}^{2}}{(n\gamma/c_{*}+\sum_{i=k+1}^{d}\lambda_{i})^{2}}+n^{-c_{0}}\,.

(340)

Next, considering again Eq. (
166
) and upper bounding the right-hand side, we get

\displaystyle n\Big(1-\frac{\gamma}{\lambda_{*}}\Big)\leq k+\frac{1}{\lambda_{*}}\sum_{i=k+1}^{d}\lambda_{i}\,.

(341)

Hence, using the assumption that the right hand side of Eq. (
339
) is upper bounded by
1/2
, which implies
k\leq n/2
,
we get

\displaystyle\lambda_{*}\leq 2\gamma+\frac{2}{n}\sum_{i=k+1}^{d}\lambda_{i}\,.

(342)

Next consider the formula for the bias term, Eq. (
168
). Denoting by
(\beta_{0,i})_{i\leq p}
the
coordinates of
{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0}
in the basis of the eigenvectors of
{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}
, we get

\displaystyle\lambda_{*}^{2}\langle{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0},({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{*}{\mathbf{I}})^{-2}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0}\rangle

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{d}\frac{\lambda_{*}^{2}\lambda_{i}\beta_{0,i}^{2}}{(\lambda_{i}+\lambda_{*})^{2}}

(343)

\displaystyle\leq\lambda_{*}^{2}\sum_{i=1}^{k}\lambda_{i}^{-1}\beta_{0,i}^{2}+\sum_{i=1}^{d}\lambda_{i}\beta_{0,i}^{2}

(344)

\displaystyle\leq 4\Big(\gamma+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=k+1}^{d}\lambda_{i}\Big)^{2}\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0,\leq k}\|_{{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{-1}}^{2}+\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0,>k}\|_{{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}}^{2}\,.

(345)

Together with Theorem
4.13
, this implies the desired bound on the bias.

Appendix B
Optimization in the linear regime

Theorem
5.1
.

Assume

\displaystyle{\rm Lip}({\boldsymbol{D}}f_{n})\,\|{\boldsymbol{y}}-f_{n}({\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\|_{2}<\frac{1}{4}\sigma^{2}_{\min}({\boldsymbol{D}}f_{n}({\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0}))\,.

(346)

Further define

\sigma_{\max}:=\sigma_{\max}({\boldsymbol{D}}f_{n}({\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})),\sigma_{\min}:=\sigma_{\min}({\boldsymbol{D}}f_{n}({\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})).

Then the following hold for all
t>0
:

1.

The empirical risk decreases exponentially fast to
0
, with rate
\lambda_{0}=\sigma^{2}_{\min}/(2n)
:

\displaystyle\widehat{L}({\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{t})\leq\widehat{L}({\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\,e^{-\lambda_{0}t}\,.

(347)

2.

The parameters stay close to the initialization and are closely tracked by those of the linearized flow.
Specifically, letting
L_{n}:={\rm Lip}({\boldsymbol{D}}f_{n})
,

\displaystyle\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{t}-{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{\sigma_{\min}}\,\|{\boldsymbol{y}}-f_{n}({\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\|_{2}\,,

(348)

\displaystyle\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{t}-\overline{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{t}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\Big\{\frac{32\sigma_{\max}}{\sigma^{2}_{\min}}\|{\boldsymbol{y}}-f_{n}({\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\|_{2}+\frac{16L_{n}}{\sigma^{3}_{\min}}\|{\boldsymbol{y}}-f_{n}({\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\|^{2}_{2}\Big\}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad{}\wedge\frac{180L_{n}\sigma_{\max}^{2}}{\sigma^{5}_{\min}}\|{\boldsymbol{y}}-f_{n}({\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\|^{2}_{2}\,.

(349)

3.

The models constructed by gradient flow and by the linearized flow are similar on test data.
Specifically, writing
f^{\mbox{\rm\tiny lin}}({\boldsymbol{\theta}})=f({\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})+{\boldsymbol{D}}f({\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})({\boldsymbol{\theta}}-{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})
, we have

\displaystyle\|f({\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{t})-f^{\mbox{\rm\tiny lin}}(\overline{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{t})\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{P})}

\displaystyle\leq\Big\{4\,{\rm Lip}({\boldsymbol{D}}f)\frac{1}{\sigma_{\min}^{2}}+180\|{\boldsymbol{D}}f({\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\|\frac{L_{n}\sigma_{\max}^{2}}{\sigma_{\min}^{5}}\Big\}\|{\boldsymbol{y}}-f_{n}({\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\|_{2}^{2}\,.

(350)

Proof.

Throughout the proof we let
L_{n}:={\rm Lip}({\boldsymbol{D}}f_{n})
, and we
use
\dot{{\boldsymbol{a}}}_{t}
to denote the derivative of quantity
{\boldsymbol{a}}_{t}
with respect to time.

Let
{\boldsymbol{y}}_{t}=f_{n}({\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{t})
. By the gradient flow equation,

\displaystyle\dot{{\boldsymbol{y}}}_{t}={\boldsymbol{D}}f_{n}({\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{t})\,\dot{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}_{t}=-\frac{1}{n}{\boldsymbol{D}}f_{n}({\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{t}){\boldsymbol{D}}f_{n}({\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{t})^{{\mathsf{T}}}({\boldsymbol{y}}_{t}-{\boldsymbol{y}})\,.

(351)

Defining the empirical kernel at time
t
,
{\boldsymbol{K}}_{t}:={\boldsymbol{D}}f_{n}({\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{t}){\boldsymbol{D}}f_{n}({\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{t})^{{\mathsf{T}}}
,
we thus have

\displaystyle\dot{{\boldsymbol{y}}}_{t}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{n}{\boldsymbol{K}}_{t}({\boldsymbol{y}}_{t}-{\boldsymbol{y}})\,,

(352)

\displaystyle\frac{{\rm d}\phantom{t}}{{\rm d}t}\|{\boldsymbol{y}}_{t}-{\boldsymbol{y}}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=-\frac{2}{n}\langle{\boldsymbol{y}}_{t}-{\boldsymbol{y}},{\boldsymbol{K}}_{t}({\boldsymbol{y}}_{t}-{\boldsymbol{y}})\rangle\,.

(353)

Letting
r_{*}:=\sigma_{\min}/(2L_{n})
and
t_{*}:=\inf\{t:\;\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{t}-{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0}\|_{2}>r_{*}\}
,
we have
\lambda_{\min}({\boldsymbol{K}}_{t})\geq(\sigma_{\min}/2)^{2}
for all
t\leq t_{*}
, whence

\displaystyle t\leq t_{*}\;\;\Rightarrow\;\;\|{\boldsymbol{y}}_{t}-{\boldsymbol{y}}\|_{2}^{2}\leq\|{\boldsymbol{y}}_{0}-{\boldsymbol{y}}\|_{2}^{2}e^{-\lambda_{0}t}\,,

(354)

with
\lambda_{0}=\sigma_{\min}^{2}/(2n)
.

Note that, for any
t\leq t_{*}
,
\sigma_{\min}({\boldsymbol{D}}f_{n}({\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{t}))\geq\sigma_{\min}/2
.
Therefore, by the gradient flow equations, for any
t\leq t_{*}
,

\displaystyle\|\dot{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{t}\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\big\|{\boldsymbol{D}}f_{n}({\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{t})^{{\mathsf{T}}}({\boldsymbol{y}}_{t}-{\boldsymbol{y}})\big\|_{2}\,,

(355)

\displaystyle\frac{{\rm d}\phantom{t}}{{\rm d}t}\|{\boldsymbol{y}}_{t}-{\boldsymbol{y}}\|_{2}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{n}\cdot\frac{\|{\boldsymbol{D}}f_{n}({\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{t})^{{\mathsf{T}}}({\boldsymbol{y}}_{t}-{\boldsymbol{y}})\big\|_{2}^{2}}{\|{\boldsymbol{y}}_{y}-{\boldsymbol{y}}\|_{2}}

(356)

\displaystyle\leq-\frac{\sigma_{\min}}{2n}\|{\boldsymbol{D}}f_{n}({\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{t})^{{\mathsf{T}}}({\boldsymbol{y}}_{t}-{\boldsymbol{y}})\big\|_{2}\,.

(357)

Therefore, by Cauchy-Schwartz,

\displaystyle\frac{{\rm d}\phantom{t}}{{\rm d}t}\Big(\|{\boldsymbol{y}}_{t}-{\boldsymbol{y}}\|_{2}+\frac{\sigma_{\min}}{2}\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{t}-{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0}\|_{2}\Big)\leq\frac{{\rm d}\phantom{t}}{{\rm d}t}\|{\boldsymbol{y}}_{t}-{\boldsymbol{y}}\|_{2}+\frac{\sigma_{\min}}{2}\|\dot{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{t}\|_{2}\leq 0\,.

(358)

This implies, for all
t\leq t_{*}
,

\displaystyle\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{t}-{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0}\|_{2}\leq\frac{2}{\sigma_{\min}}\|{\boldsymbol{y}}-{\boldsymbol{y}}_{0}\|_{2}\,.

(359)

Assume by contradiction
t_{*}<\infty
. The last equation together with the assumption (
346
)
implies
\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{t_{*}}-{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0}\|_{2}<r_{*}
, which contradicts the definition of
t_{*}
. We conclude that
t_{*}=\infty
,
and Eq. (
347
) follows from Eq. (
354
).

Equation (
348
) follows from Eq. (
359
).

In order to prove Eq. (
349
), let
\overline{\boldsymbol{y}}_{t}:=f_{n}({\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})+{\boldsymbol{D}}f_{n}({\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})(\overline{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{t}-{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})
.
Note that this satisfies an equation similar to (
352
), namely

\displaystyle\dot{\overline{\boldsymbol{y}}}_{t}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{n}{\boldsymbol{K}}_{0}(\overline{\boldsymbol{y}}_{t}-{\boldsymbol{y}})\,.

(360)

Define the difference
{\boldsymbol{r}}_{t}:={\boldsymbol{y}}_{t}-\overline{\boldsymbol{y}}_{t}
. We then have
\dot{{\boldsymbol{r}}}_{t}=-({\boldsymbol{K}}_{t}/n){\boldsymbol{r}}_{t}-(({\boldsymbol{K}}_{t}-{\boldsymbol{K}}_{0})/n)(\overline{\boldsymbol{y}}_{t}-{\boldsymbol{y}})
,
whence

\displaystyle\frac{{\rm d}\phantom{t}}{{\rm d}t}\|{\boldsymbol{r}}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=-\frac{2}{n}\langle{\boldsymbol{r}}_{t},{\boldsymbol{K}}_{t}{\boldsymbol{r}}_{t}\rangle-\frac{2}{n}\langle{\boldsymbol{r}}_{t},({\boldsymbol{K}}_{t}-{\boldsymbol{K}}_{0})(\overline{\boldsymbol{y}}_{t}-{\boldsymbol{y}})\rangle

(361)

\displaystyle\leq-\frac{2}{n}\lambda_{\min}({\boldsymbol{K}}_{t})\|{\boldsymbol{r}}_{t}\|^{2}_{2}+\frac{2}{n}\|{\boldsymbol{r}}_{t}\|_{2}\big\|{\boldsymbol{K}}_{t}-{\boldsymbol{K}}_{0}\big\|\|\overline{\boldsymbol{y}}_{t}-{\boldsymbol{y}}\|_{2}\,.

(362)

Using
2\lambda_{\min}({\boldsymbol{K}}_{t})/n\geq\lambda_{0}
and
\|\overline{\boldsymbol{y}}_{t}-{\boldsymbol{y}}_{t}\|_{2}\leq\|{\boldsymbol{y}}_{0}-{\boldsymbol{y}}\|_{2}e^{-\lambda_{0}t/2}
, we get

\displaystyle\frac{{\rm d}\phantom{t}}{{\rm d}t}\|{\boldsymbol{r}}_{t}\|_{2}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\lambda_{0}}{2}\|{\boldsymbol{r}}_{t}\|_{2}+\frac{1}{n}\big\|{\boldsymbol{K}}_{t}-{\boldsymbol{K}}_{0}\big\|\|{\boldsymbol{y}}_{0}-{\boldsymbol{y}}\|_{2}\,e^{-\lambda_{0}t/2}\,.

(363)

Note that

\displaystyle\big\|{\boldsymbol{K}}_{t}-{\boldsymbol{K}}_{0}\big\|

\displaystyle=\big\|{\boldsymbol{D}}f_{n}({\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{t}){\boldsymbol{D}}f_{n}({\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{t})^{{\mathsf{T}}}-{\boldsymbol{D}}f_{n}({\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0}){\boldsymbol{D}}f_{n}({\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})^{{\mathsf{T}}}\big\|

(364)

\displaystyle\leq 2\big\|{\boldsymbol{D}}f_{n}({\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\big\|\big\|{\boldsymbol{D}}f_{n}({\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{t})-{\boldsymbol{D}}f_{n}({\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\big\|+\big\|{\boldsymbol{D}}f_{n}({\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{t})-{\boldsymbol{D}}f_{n}({\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\big\|^{2}

(365)

\displaystyle\leq 2\sigma_{\max}L_{n}\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{t}-{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0}\|+L_{n}^{2}\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{t}-{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0}\|^{2}

(366)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{5}{2}\sigma_{\max}L_{n}\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{t}-{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0}\|\,.

(367)

(In the last inequality, we used the fact that
L_{n}\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{t}-{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0}\|\leq\sigma_{\min}/2

by definition of
r_{*}
.)
Applying Grönwall’s inequality, and using
{\boldsymbol{r}}_{0}=0
, we obtain

\displaystyle\|{\boldsymbol{r}}_{t}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq e^{-\lambda_{0}t/2}\|{\boldsymbol{y}}_{0}-{\boldsymbol{y}}\|_{2}\int_{0}^{t}\frac{1}{n}\big\|{\boldsymbol{K}}_{s}-{\boldsymbol{K}}_{0}\big\|{\rm d}s

(368)

\displaystyle\leq e^{-\lambda_{0}t/2}t\|{\boldsymbol{y}}_{0}-{\boldsymbol{y}}\|_{2}\sup_{s\in[0,t]}\frac{1}{n}\big\|{\boldsymbol{K}}_{s}-{\boldsymbol{K}}_{0}\big\|

(369)

\displaystyle\leq e^{-\lambda_{0}t/4}\frac{2}{\lambda_{0}}\|{\boldsymbol{y}}_{0}-{\boldsymbol{y}}\|_{2}\sup_{s\geq 0}\frac{1}{n}\big\|{\boldsymbol{K}}_{s}-{\boldsymbol{K}}_{0}\big\|

(370)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{\leq}}e^{-\lambda_{0}t/4}\frac{2}{\lambda_{0}}\|{\boldsymbol{y}}_{0}-{\boldsymbol{y}}\|_{2}\frac{5}{2n}L_{n}\sigma_{\max}\sup_{s\geq 0}\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{s}-{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0}\|_{2}

(371)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(b)}}{{\leq}}e^{-\lambda_{0}t/4}\frac{2}{\lambda_{0}}\|{\boldsymbol{y}}_{0}-{\boldsymbol{y}}\|_{2}\frac{5}{2n}L_{n}\sigma_{\max}\cdot\frac{2}{\sigma_{\min}}\|{\boldsymbol{y}}_{0}-{\boldsymbol{y}}\|_{2}

(372)

\displaystyle\leq 20\,e^{-\lambda_{0}t/4}\frac{\sigma_{\max}}{\sigma_{\min}^{3}}L_{n}\|{\boldsymbol{y}}-{\boldsymbol{y}}_{0}\|_{2}^{2}\,.

(373)

Here in
(a)
we used Eq. (
367
) and in
(b)
Eq. (
359
).
Further using
\|{\boldsymbol{r}}_{t}\|_{2}\leq\|{\boldsymbol{y}}_{t}-{\boldsymbol{y}}\|_{2}+\|\overline{\boldsymbol{y}}_{t}-{\boldsymbol{y}}\|_{2}\leq 2\|{\boldsymbol{y}}_{0}-{\boldsymbol{y}}\|\exp(-\lambda_{0}t/2)
, we get

\displaystyle\|{\boldsymbol{y}}_{t}-\overline{\boldsymbol{y}}_{t}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2e^{-\lambda_{0}t/4}\|{\boldsymbol{y}}-{\boldsymbol{y}}_{0}\|_{2}\Big\{1\wedge\frac{10\sigma_{\max}}{\sigma_{\min}^{3}}L_{n}\|{\boldsymbol{y}}-{\boldsymbol{y}}_{0}\|_{2}\Big\}\,.

(374)

Recall the gradient flow equations for
{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{t}
and
\overline{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{t}
:

\displaystyle\dot{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}_{t}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}{\boldsymbol{D}}f_{n}({\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{t})^{{\mathsf{T}}}({\boldsymbol{y}}-{\boldsymbol{y}}_{t})\,,

(375)

\displaystyle\dot{\overline{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}_{t}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}{\boldsymbol{D}}f_{n}({\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})^{{\mathsf{T}}}({\boldsymbol{y}}-\overline{\boldsymbol{y}}_{t})\,.

(376)

Taking the difference of these equations, we get

\displaystyle\frac{{\rm d}\phantom{t}}{{\rm d}t}\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{t}-\overline{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{t}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{n}\big\|{\boldsymbol{D}}f_{n}({\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{t})-{\boldsymbol{D}}f_{n}({\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\big\|\|{\boldsymbol{y}}_{t}-{\boldsymbol{y}}\|_{2}+\frac{1}{n}\big\|{\boldsymbol{D}}f_{n}({\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\big\|\|{\boldsymbol{y}}_{t}-\overline{\boldsymbol{y}}_{t}\|_{2}

(377)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{L_{n}}{n}\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{t}-{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0}\|_{2}\|{\boldsymbol{y}}_{t}-{\boldsymbol{y}}\|_{2}+\frac{\sigma_{\max}}{n}\|{\boldsymbol{y}}_{t}-\overline{\boldsymbol{y}}_{t}\|_{2}

(378)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{\leq}}\frac{L_{n}}{n}\cdot\frac{2}{\sigma_{\min}}\,\|{\boldsymbol{y}}-{\boldsymbol{y}}_{0}\|_{2}^{2}e^{-\lambda_{0}t/2}+\frac{\sigma_{\max}}{n}\cdot 2e^{-\lambda_{0}t/4}\|{\boldsymbol{y}}-{\boldsymbol{y}}_{0}\|_{2}\Big\{1\wedge\frac{10\sigma_{\max}}{\sigma_{\min}^{3}}L_{n}\|{\boldsymbol{y}}-{\boldsymbol{y}}_{0}\|_{2}\Big\}

(379)

where in
(a)
we used Eqs. (
348
), (
354
) and (
374
).
Integrating the last expression (thanks to
\overline{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0}={\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0}
), we get

\displaystyle\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{t}-\overline{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{t}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{8L_{n}}{\sigma_{\min}^{3}}\|{\boldsymbol{y}}-{\boldsymbol{y}}_{0}\|_{2}^{2}+\Big\{\frac{16\sigma_{\max}}{\sigma_{\min}^{2}}\|{\boldsymbol{y}}-{\boldsymbol{y}}_{0}\|_{2}\wedge\frac{160\sigma^{2}_{\max}}{\sigma_{\min}^{5}}L_{n}\|{\boldsymbol{y}}-{\boldsymbol{y}}_{0}\|_{2}^{2}\Big\}\,.

(380)

Simplifying, we get Eq. (
349
).

Finally, to prove Eq. (
350
), write

\displaystyle\|f({\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{t})-f_{\mbox{\rm\tiny lin}}(\overline{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{t})\|_{L^{2}}\leq\underbrace{\|f({\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{t})-f_{\mbox{\rm\tiny lin}}({\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{t})\|_{L^{2}}}_{E_{1}}+\underbrace{\|f_{\mbox{\rm\tiny lin}}({\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{t})-f_{\mbox{\rm\tiny lin}}(\overline{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{t})\|_{L^{2}}}_{E_{2}}\,.

(381)

By writing
f({\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{t})-f_{\mbox{\rm\tiny lin}}({\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{t})=\int_{0}^{t}\frac{{\rm d}\phantom{s}}{{\rm d}s}[f({\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{s})-f_{\mbox{\rm\tiny lin}}({\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{s})]{\rm d}s
, we get

\displaystyle E_{1}

\displaystyle=\left\|\int_{0}^{t}[{\boldsymbol{D}}f({\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{s})-{\boldsymbol{D}}f({\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})]\dot{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}_{s}{\rm d}s\right\|_{L^{2}}

(382)

\displaystyle\leq{\rm Lip}({\boldsymbol{D}}f)\sup_{s\geq 0}\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{s}-{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0}\|_{2}\int_{0}^{t}\|\dot{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}_{s}\|_{2}{\rm d}s

(383)

\displaystyle\leq{\rm Lip}({\boldsymbol{D}}f)\cdot\frac{4\|{\boldsymbol{y}}-{\boldsymbol{y}}_{0}\|_{2}^{2}}{\sigma_{\min}^{2}}\,.

(384)

In the last step we used Eq. (
348
) and noted that the same argument to prove the latter indeed
also bounds the integral
\int_{0}^{t}\|\dot{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}_{s}\|_{2}{\rm d}s
(see Eq. (
358
)).

Finally, to bound term
E_{2}
, note that
f_{\mbox{\rm\tiny lin}}({\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{t})-f_{\mbox{\rm\tiny lin}}(\overline{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{t})={\boldsymbol{D}}f({\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})({\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{t}-\overline{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{t})
,
and using Eq. (
349
), we get

\displaystyle E_{2}\leq 180\|{\boldsymbol{D}}f({\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\|\frac{L_{n}\sigma_{\max}^{2}}{\sigma_{\min}^{5}}\|{\boldsymbol{y}}-{\boldsymbol{y}}_{0}\|_{2}^{2}\,.

(385)

Equation (
350
) follows by putting together the above bounds for
E_{1}
and
E_{2}
.
∎

We next pass to the case of two-layers networks:

\displaystyle f({\boldsymbol{x}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}):=\frac{\alpha}{\sqrt{m}}\sum_{j=1}^{m}b_{j}\sigma(\langle{\boldsymbol{w}}_{j},{\boldsymbol{x}}\rangle),~~{\boldsymbol{\theta}}=({\boldsymbol{w}}_{1},\dots,{\boldsymbol{w}}_{m})\,.

(386)

Lemma
5.3
.

Under Assumption 5.2, further assume

\{(y_{i},{\boldsymbol{x}}_{i})\}_{i\leq n}
to be i.i.d. with
{\boldsymbol{x}}_{i}\sim_{iid}{\sf N}(0,{\mathbf{I}}_{d})
, and
y_{i}

B^{2}
-sub-Gaussian.
Then there exist constants
C_{i}
,
depending uniquely on
\sigma
, such that the following hold with probability at least
1-2\exp\{-n/C_{0}\}
,
provided
md\geq C_{0}n\log n
,
n\leq d^{\ell_{0}}
(whenever not specified, these hold for both

{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0}\in\{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0}^{(1)},{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0}^{(2)}\}
):

\displaystyle\|{\boldsymbol{y}}-f_{n}({\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{(1)}_{0})\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C_{1}\big(B+\alpha)\sqrt{n}\,

(387)

\displaystyle\|{\boldsymbol{y}}-f_{n}({\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{(2)}_{0})\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C_{1}B\sqrt{n}\,,

(388)

\displaystyle\sigma_{\min}({\boldsymbol{D}}f_{n}({\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0}))

\displaystyle\geq C_{2}\alpha\sqrt{d}\,,

(389)

\displaystyle\sigma_{\max}({\boldsymbol{D}}f_{n}({\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0}))

\displaystyle\leq C_{3}\alpha\big(\sqrt{n}+\sqrt{d}\big)\,,

(390)

\displaystyle{\rm Lip}({\boldsymbol{D}}f_{n})

\displaystyle\leq C_{4}\alpha\sqrt{\frac{d}{m}}\big(\sqrt{n}+\sqrt{d}\big)\,.

(391)

Further

\displaystyle\|{\boldsymbol{D}}f({\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\|

\displaystyle\leq C_{1}^{\prime}\alpha\,,

(392)

\displaystyle{\rm Lip}({\boldsymbol{D}}f)

\displaystyle\leq C_{4}^{\prime}\alpha\sqrt{\frac{d}{m}}\,.

(393)

Proof.

Since the
y_{i}
are
B^{2}
sub-Gaussian, we have
\|{\boldsymbol{y}}\|_{2}\leq C_{1}B\sqrt{n}
with the stated probability.
Equation (
388
) follows since by construction
f_{n}({\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{(2)}_{0})=0
.

For Eq. (
387
) we claim that
\|f_{n}({\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0}^{(1)})\|_{2}\leq C_{1}\alpha\sqrt{n}
with the claimed probability.
To show this, it is sufficient of course to consider
\alpha=1
. Let
F({\boldsymbol{X}},{\boldsymbol{W}}):=\|f_{n}({\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0}^{(1)})\|_{2}
, where

{\boldsymbol{X}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n\times d}
contains as rows the vectors
{\boldsymbol{x}}_{i}
, and
{\boldsymbol{W}}
the vectors
{\boldsymbol{w}}_{i}
.
We also write

{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0}^{(1)}={\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0}
for simplicity. We
have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\{F({\boldsymbol{X}},{\boldsymbol{W}})\}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\{\|f_{n}({\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\|_{2}^{2}\}=n\mathbb{E}\{f({\boldsymbol{x}}_{1};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})^{2}\}

(394)

\displaystyle=n\text{Var}\{\sigma(\langle{\boldsymbol{w}}_{1},{\boldsymbol{x}}_{1}\rangle)\}\leq Cn\,.

(395)

Next, proceeding as in the proof of
[
OS20
, Lemma 7]

(letting
{\boldsymbol{b}}=(b_{j})_{j\leq m}
)

\displaystyle\big|F({\boldsymbol{X}},{\boldsymbol{W}}_{1})-F({\boldsymbol{X}},{\boldsymbol{W}}_{2})\big|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}}\big\|\sigma({\boldsymbol{X}}{\boldsymbol{W}}_{1}^{{\mathsf{T}}}){\boldsymbol{b}}-\sigma({\boldsymbol{X}}{\boldsymbol{W}}_{2}^{{\mathsf{T}}}){\boldsymbol{b}}\big\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\big\|\sigma({\boldsymbol{X}}{\boldsymbol{W}}_{1}^{{\mathsf{T}}})-\sigma({\boldsymbol{X}}{\boldsymbol{W}}_{2}^{{\mathsf{T}}})\big\|_{F}

\displaystyle\leq C\big\|{\boldsymbol{X}}{\boldsymbol{W}}_{1}^{{\mathsf{T}}}-{\boldsymbol{X}}{\boldsymbol{W}}_{2}^{{\mathsf{T}}}\big\|_{F}

\displaystyle\leq C\|{\boldsymbol{X}}\|\big\|{\boldsymbol{W}}_{1}^{{\mathsf{T}}}-{\boldsymbol{W}}_{2}^{{\mathsf{T}}}\big\|_{F}\,.

We have
\|{\boldsymbol{X}}\|\leq 2(\sqrt{n}+\sqrt{d})
with the probability at least
1-2\exp\{-(n\vee d)/C)

[
Ver18
]
.
On this event,
F({\boldsymbol{X}},\,\cdot\,)
is
2(\sqrt{n}+\sqrt{d})
-Lipschitz with respect to
{\boldsymbol{W}}
. Recall that the uniform measure on
the sphere of radius
\sqrt{d}
satisfies a log-Sobolev inequality with
\Theta(1)
constant,
[
Led01
, Chapter 5]
,
that the log-Sobolev constant for a product measure is the same as the worst constant of each of the terms.
We then have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(F({\boldsymbol{X}},{\boldsymbol{W}})\geq\mathbb{E}F({\boldsymbol{X}},{\boldsymbol{W}})+t)\leq e^{-dt^{2}/C(n+d)}+2e^{-(n\vee d)/C}\,.

(396)

Taking
t=C_{1}\sqrt{n}
for a sufficiently large constant
C_{1}
implies that the right-hand
side is at most
2\exp(-(n\vee d)/C)
, which proves the claim.

Notice that all the following inequalities are homogeneous in

\alpha>0
. Hence, we will assume—without loss of
generality—that
\alpha=1
.
Equation (
389
) follows from
[
OS20
, Lemma 4]
. Indeed this lemma implies

\displaystyle m\geq\frac{C(n+d)\log n}{d\lambda_{\min}({\boldsymbol{K}})}\;\;\;\Rightarrow\;\;\;\sigma_{\min}({\boldsymbol{D}}f_{n}({\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0}))\geq c_{0}\sqrt{d\lambda_{\min}({\boldsymbol{K}})}\,,

(397)

where
{\boldsymbol{K}}
is the empirical NT kernel

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{K}}=\frac{1}{d}\mathbb{E}\big\{{\boldsymbol{D}}f_{n}({\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0}){\boldsymbol{D}}f_{n}({\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\big\}=\big(K_{{\sf NT}}({\boldsymbol{x}}_{i},{\boldsymbol{x}}_{j})\big)_{i,j\leq n}\,.

(398)

Under Assumption 5.2 (in particular
\sigma^{\prime}
having non-vanishing Hermite coefficients
\mu_{\ell}(\sigma)

for all
\ell\leq\ell_{0}
), and
n\leq d^{\ell_{0}}
, we have
\lambda_{\min}({\boldsymbol{K}})\geq c_{0}
with the stated probability,
see for instance
[
EK10
]
. This implies the claim.

For Eq. (
390
), note that, for any vector
{\boldsymbol{v}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
,
\|{\boldsymbol{v}}\|_{2}=1

we have

\displaystyle\big\|{\boldsymbol{D}}f_{n}({\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})^{{\mathsf{T}}}{\boldsymbol{v}}\big\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{m}\sum_{i,j\leq n}\sum_{\ell=1}^{m}v_{i}\sigma^{\prime}(\langle{\boldsymbol{w}}_{\ell},{\boldsymbol{x}}_{i}\rangle)v_{j}\sigma^{\prime}(\langle{\boldsymbol{w}}_{\ell},{\boldsymbol{x}}_{j}\rangle)\langle{\boldsymbol{x}}_{i},{\boldsymbol{x}}_{j}\rangle

(399)

\displaystyle=\langle{\boldsymbol{M}},{\boldsymbol{X}},{\boldsymbol{X}}^{{\mathsf{T}}}\rangle\,,

(400)

\displaystyle M_{ij}

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{m}\sum_{\ell=1}^{m}v_{i}\sigma^{\prime}(\langle{\boldsymbol{w}}_{\ell},{\boldsymbol{x}}_{i}\rangle)v_{j}\sigma^{\prime}(\langle{\boldsymbol{w}}_{\ell},{\boldsymbol{x}}_{j}\rangle)\,.

(401)

Since
{\boldsymbol{M}}\succeq 0
, we have

\displaystyle\big\|{\boldsymbol{D}}f_{n}({\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})^{{\mathsf{T}}}{\boldsymbol{v}}\big\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq{\mathsf{tr}}({\boldsymbol{M}})\|{\boldsymbol{X}}\|^{2}

(402)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{m}\sum_{\ell=1}^{m}\sum_{i=1}^{n}v^{2}_{i}\sigma^{\prime}(\langle{\boldsymbol{w}}_{\ell},{\boldsymbol{x}}_{i}\rangle)^{2}\,\cdot\,\|{\boldsymbol{X}}\|^{2}

(403)

\displaystyle\leq B^{2}\|{\boldsymbol{v}}\|^{2}_{2}\|{\boldsymbol{X}}\|^{2}\,.

(404)

Hence
\sigma_{\max}({\boldsymbol{D}}f_{n}({\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0}))\leq B\|{\boldsymbol{X}}\|
and
the claim follows from standard estimates of
operator norms of random matrices with independent entries.

Equation (
391
) follows from
[
OS20
, Lemma 5]
, which yields
(after adapting to the different normalization of the
{\boldsymbol{x}}_{i}
, and using the fact that
\max_{i\leq n}\|{\boldsymbol{x}}_{i}\|_{2}\leq C\sqrt{d}

with probability at least
1-2\exp(-d/C)
):

\displaystyle\big\|{\boldsymbol{D}}f_{n}({\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{1})-{\boldsymbol{D}}f_{n}({\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{2})\big\|\leq C\sqrt{\frac{d}{m}}\|{\boldsymbol{X}}\|\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{1}-{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{2}\|_{2}\,.

(Here
\|{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{1}-{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{2}\|_{2}=\|{\boldsymbol{W}}_{1}-{\boldsymbol{W}}_{2}\|_{F}
, where
{\boldsymbol{W}}_{i}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{m\times d}
is the matrix
whose rows are the weight vectors.) The claim follows once more by using
\|{\boldsymbol{X}}\|\leq 2(\sqrt{n}+\sqrt{d})
with
probability at least
1-2\exp\{-(n\vee d)/C)
.

In order to prove Eq. (
392
), note that, for
h\in L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{d},\mathbb{P})
,

\displaystyle\big\|{\boldsymbol{D}}f({\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})^{*}h\big\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\{Q_{h}({\boldsymbol{x}}_{1},{\boldsymbol{x}}_{2})\,P({\boldsymbol{x}}_{1},{\boldsymbol{x}}_{2})\}\,,

(405)

\displaystyle Q_{h}({\boldsymbol{x}}_{1},{\boldsymbol{x}}_{2})

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{m}\sum_{\ell=1}^{m}\sigma^{\prime}(\langle{\boldsymbol{w}}_{\ell},{\boldsymbol{x}}_{1}\rangle)h({\boldsymbol{x}}_{1})\sigma^{\prime}(\langle{\boldsymbol{w}}_{\ell},{\boldsymbol{x}}_{2}\rangle)h({\boldsymbol{x}}_{2})\,,

(406)

\displaystyle P({\boldsymbol{x}}_{1},{\boldsymbol{x}}_{2})

\displaystyle:=\langle{\boldsymbol{x}}_{1},{\boldsymbol{x}}_{2}\rangle\,.

(407)

Here expectation is with respect to independent random vectors
{\boldsymbol{x}}_{1},{\boldsymbol{x}}_{2}\sim\mathbb{P}
. Denote
by
{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{h}
and
{\boldsymbol{P}}
the integral operators in
L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{d},\mathbb{P})
with kernels
Q_{h}
and
P
.
It is easy to see that
{\boldsymbol{P}}
is the projector onto the subspace of linear functions, and
{\boldsymbol{Q}}_{h}

is positive semidefinite. Therefore

\displaystyle\big\|{\boldsymbol{D}}f({\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})^{*}h\big\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq{\mathsf{tr}}({\boldsymbol{Q}}_{h})=\frac{1}{m}\sum_{\ell=1}^{m}\mathbb{E}\big\{\sigma^{\prime}(\langle{\boldsymbol{w}}_{\ell},{\boldsymbol{x}}_{1}\rangle)^{2}h({\boldsymbol{x}}_{1})^{2}\big\}

(408)

\displaystyle\leq B^{2}\|h\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\,.

(409)

This implies
\|{\boldsymbol{D}}f({\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\|\leq B
.

In order to prove Eq. (
393
), define

\Delta_{\ell}({\boldsymbol{x}}):=\sigma^{\prime}(\langle{\boldsymbol{w}}_{1,\ell},{\boldsymbol{x}}\rangle)-\sigma^{\prime}(\langle{\boldsymbol{w}}_{2,\ell},{\boldsymbol{x}}\rangle)
.
Let
h\in L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{d},\mathbb{P})
and note that

\displaystyle\big\|{\boldsymbol{D}}f({\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})^{*}h\big\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{m}\sum_{\ell=1}^{m}\Big\|\mathbb{E}\big\{{\boldsymbol{x}}h({\boldsymbol{x}})\Delta_{\ell}({\boldsymbol{x}})\big\}\Big\|_{2}^{2}

(410)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{m}\sum_{\ell=1}^{m}\mathbb{E}\big\{\|{\boldsymbol{x}}\|\,|h({\boldsymbol{x}})\Delta_{\ell}({\boldsymbol{x}})|\big\}^{2}

(411)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{m}\sum_{\ell=1}^{m}\mathbb{E}\big\{\|{\boldsymbol{x}}\|^{2}\,\Delta_{\ell}({\boldsymbol{x}})^{2}\big\}\,\|h\|_{L^{2}}\,.

(412)

Note that
|\Delta_{\ell}({\boldsymbol{x}})|\leq B\,|\langle{\boldsymbol{w}}_{1,\ell}-{\boldsymbol{w}}_{2,\ell},{\boldsymbol{x}}\rangle|
. Using this and the last expression above, we get

\displaystyle\big\|{\boldsymbol{D}}f({\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\big\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{B^{2}}{m}\sum_{\ell=1}^{m}\mathbb{E}\big\{\|{\boldsymbol{x}}\|^{2}\,\langle{\boldsymbol{x}},{\boldsymbol{w}}_{1,\ell}-{\boldsymbol{w}}_{2,\ell}\rangle^{2}\big\}

(413)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{B^{2}}{m}(d+2)\sum_{\ell=1}^{m}\|{\boldsymbol{w}}_{1,\ell}-{\boldsymbol{w}}_{2,\ell}\|_{2}^{2}=\frac{B^{2}}{m}(d+2)\|{\boldsymbol{W}}_{1}-{\boldsymbol{W}}_{2}\|_{F}^{2}\,,

(414)

where the second inequality follows from the Gaussian identity
\mathbb{E}\{\|{\boldsymbol{x}}\|^{2}{\boldsymbol{x}}{\boldsymbol{x}}^{{\mathsf{T}}}\}=(d+2){\mathbf{I}}_{d}
.
This proves Eq. (
393
).
∎

Theorem
5.4
.

Consider the two layer neural network of (
386
)
under the assumptions of Lemma
5.3
.
Further let
\overline{\alpha}:=\alpha/(1+\alpha)
for initialization
{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0}={\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0}^{(1)}
and

\overline{\alpha}:=\alpha
for
{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0}={\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0}^{(2)}
.
Then there exist constants
C_{i}
,
depending uniquely on
\sigma
, such that
if
md\geq C_{0}n\log n
,
d\leq n\leq d^{\ell_{0}}
and

\displaystyle\overline{\alpha}\geq C_{0}\sqrt{\frac{n^{2}}{md}}\,,

(415)

then,
with probability at least
1-2\exp\{-n/C_{0}\}
,
the following hold for all
t\geq 0
.

1.

Gradient flow converges exponentially fast to a global
minimizer. Specifically, letting

\lambda_{*}=C_{1}\alpha^{2}d/n
, we have

\displaystyle\widehat{L}({\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{t})\leq\widehat{L}({\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\,e^{-\lambda_{*}t}\,.

(416)

2.

The model constructed by gradient flow and linearized flow are similar on test data,
namely

\displaystyle\|f({\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{t})-f_{\mbox{\rm\tiny lin}}(\overline{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{t})\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{P})}\leq C_{1}\left\{\frac{\alpha}{\overline{\alpha}^{2}}\sqrt{\frac{n^{2}}{md}}+\frac{1}{\overline{\alpha}^{2}}\sqrt{\frac{n^{5}}{md^{4}}}\right\}\,.

(417)

Proof.

Throughout the proof, we use
C
to denote constants depending only on
\sigma
, that might change from line to line.
Using Lemma
5.3
, the condition (
346
) reads

\displaystyle\alpha\sqrt{\frac{dn}{m}}\cdot\frac{\alpha}{\overline{\alpha}}\sqrt{n}\leq C\big(\alpha\sqrt{d}\big)^{2}\,.

(418)

which is equivalent to Eq. (
415
). We can therefore apply Theorem
5.1
.

Equation (
416
) follows from Theorem
5.1
, point 1,
using the lower bound on
\sigma_{\min}
given in Eq. (
389
).

Equation (
417
) follows from Theorem
5.1
, point 3, using the
estimates in Lemma
5.3
.
∎
